# keenetic-control-center - Work Plan

## TL;DR (For humans)
<!-- Fill this LAST, after the detailed plan below is written, so it summarizes the REAL plan. -->
<!-- Plain English for a non-engineer: NO file paths, NO todo numbers, NO wave/agent/tool names. -->

**Who this is for and what changes for them:** Owners of Keenetic and Netcraze routers (firmware 4.3 LTS and newer) get one place, mainly a web page in their own language, to see and change every setting of all their routers, keep backups, and move all settings from an old router to a new one safely. AI assistants and scripts get the same powers through an MCP server and a command-line tool.

**What you'll get:** A single program (`kcc`) for Linux, macOS, Windows and Docker that serves a six-language web control center (English, Russian, Ukrainian, German, Belarusian, Kazakh) with logins and roles, a fleet dashboard, pages for all everyday router areas, a command explorer that reaches every documented router command, backups with diffs, and a step-by-step migration wizard. The same engine also powers an MCP server for AI agents and a scriptable CLI, exports router and app metrics to Prometheus (with a ready Grafana dashboard and alerts), forwards router logs to Loki or Elasticsearch/OpenSearch, refuses to run with root/sudo or extra privileges, saves reusable settings presets (DoH/DoT, DNS, routing, firewall, VPN clients, Wi-Fi, baseline, users, schedules) captured from one router and applied to another, and ships as a hardened image for rootless Podman with its own seccomp profile.

**Why this approach:** The router's official API mirrors its command-line reference one-to-one but cannot describe itself, so we turn the official per-model reference manuals into a built-in command catalog and check every request against it. This gives full coverage and stops the router's worst habit: silently ignoring a misspelled setting. Every change is read back, nothing is saved without your say-so, and risky changes and migrations run under the router's own auto-rollback timer, so a mistake that cuts you off undoes itself.

**What it will NOT do:** It will not modify firmware, install anything on the router, or use Keenetic's cloud. It will not save router changes on its own or skip certificate checks. It will not migrate things tied to the old device (KeenDNS registration, certificates, MAC addresses, LED/USB hardware settings); those are listed in the plan with the reason.

**Effort:** XL
**Risk:** Medium - Several router behaviours rest on one source each: replaying settings one line at a time, admin password hashes being tied to the model name, the fail-safe timer's exact behaviour, and whether the manuals can be turned into data reliably. Two early experiments and a pass/fail check on the catalog settle these first, each with a fallback agreed in advance.
**Decisions to sanity-check:**
- Admin/user passwords are re-entered during migration instead of copied.
- In a full migration, Wi-Fi/PPP passwords and WireGuard keys are copied as-is (the new router replaces the old one); presets instead regenerate WireGuard keys by default.
- The command catalog stores only command structure, with page links to Keenetic's PDFs and none of their text (the manuals are copyrighted and carry no license).
- AI agents see a small core set of at most 25 tools by default; more sets are switched on in config.
- Live tests only touch routers whose serial numbers you list.
- Migration and presets only go onto the same or newer firmware than the source, and expect a clean new router (a non-clean one only gets a warning).
- Presets copy settings groups one source -> one target per run; WireGuard keys are regenerated on the target by default (you register the new public key on your VPN server).
- Firmware 4.3 LTS and 5.0 are supported from the official manuals and tested only against the simulator (your hardware runs 5.1+); kcc labels them "not verified on real hardware" and asks for confirmation before migrating onto them.
- Router logs are pulled through the router API (no syslog listener); anything still buffered when kcc stops, or beyond a 10 000-line buffer while a log server is down, is lost and counted.
- Root, `sudo` and Windows elevation are refused with no override flag, as in krb-passwd-ui.
- Metrics are served on a separate, unauthenticated, loopback-only listener (how rmcp-server-kit does it); per-device metrics are off by default.
- Router passwords are stored encrypted in the app's state folder (no OS keychain).
- Web login uses local accounts, with OIDC possible later.

Your next move: review the summary; then start execution with `$ulw-execute keenetic-control-center` (options below). Full execution detail follows below.

---

> TL;DR (machine): XL, medium risk; 60 todos in 12 waves (2 Wave-1 spikes) + 4 final checks; firmware 4.3+, Prometheus exporter, Loki/Elastic log forwarding, privilege floor, rootless Podman + seccomp; deliverables: kcc binary (Web UI main, MCP, CLI), RCI client with ndw2/ndw4/token/digest, generated command catalog, 21 curated domains, migration engine, 6 locales, strict-lint CI, release builds + OCI image.

## Scope
### Affected user and ideal state
<!-- From the draft ledger: who this touches and how they use the result, then one IS row per property with its reason, then one GAP row per difference with its reason. Every todo below closes a GAP row; ## Success criteria proves every IS row. -->
**Affected user:**
- **U1 Router owner/admin (primary)** - manages one or more Keenetic/Netcraze routers (KeeneticOS/NetcrazeOS 4.3+: 4.3 LTS, 5.0, 5.1, 5.2) from a browser through the **Web UI, the main feature**: fleet overview, every setting the router exposes, backups, and moving settings from an old router to a new one. Today: the stock per-router web UI, no fleet view, no migration path between models, no access to CLI-only settings without telnet/SSH.
- **U2 AI agent** (Claude Code / Codex / Cursor / OpenCode) using the integrated **MCP server** (secondary feature) to read and change router state safely.
- **U3 Operator** deploying `kcc` as a service (binary or OCI image, TLS, local accounts, API keys, RBAC).
- **U4 Scripter** using the `kcc` CLI (human or `--json` output).
- **U5 Maintainers/agents** working in this repo under andrico21/rust-guidelines (strictest lint profile).
- **U7 Monitoring operator** - scrapes router and kcc metrics with Prometheus/Grafana and ships router logs to Loki or Elasticsearch/OpenSearch.
- **U6 Household members** behind the router - they never touch kcc, but lose internet whenever a fail-safe expiry or reboot-class action restarts the router.

| Row | Statement | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| IS-1 | `kcc` connects to any KeeneticOS/NetcrazeOS 4.3+ router (4.3 LTS, 5.0, 5.1, 5.2; the owner's only hardware runs 5.1+, so 4.3 LTS and 5.0 are a documentation-based support tier - built from the official manuals, tested only against the fake router, and labelled as such to the user) (LAN, or KeenDNS HTTP-proxy name) using whichever scheme the firmware advertises - `x-ndw2-interactive`, `x-ndw4-interactive` (SCRAM-SHA3-512 + Argon2id), `X-NDMA-TKN` token, or Digest via port 79 - and re-authenticates transparently when the 300 s sliding session lapses | 5.2 adds ndw4 + tokens and NDMS devs announced removal of ndw2; sessions expire after 5 idle minutes |
| IS-2 | Every RCI resource documented for the connected model (settings, actions, background processes, `show`, `show rc/sc`, `no`/DELETE) is reachable from the Web UI command explorer, the MCP generic tools and the CLI, and every request is validated against the command catalog before it is sent | "Full API coverage"; the router answers a mistyped field with a silent `{}` no-op |
| IS-3 | The ~21 everyday domains (system, interfaces, Wi-Fi, mesh, clients, DHCP, segments, routes, policies, NAT/port forwarding, firewall v4/v6, DNS, VPN clients, VPN servers, users, services, components/firmware, storage, schedules, KeenDNS, diagnostics/logs) have typed pages/tools whose every write is read back and verified, with RCI errors inside HTTP 200 surfaced | Router accepts wrong commands silently; a guessed payload once wiped all static routes on a production router |
| IS-4 | Nothing is persisted on the router without an explicit save (commands the router itself persists immediately, e.g. `system country`, are labelled as such before they run); a local backup of startup-config is taken before the first change of every change set; risky applies run under the router's fail-safe timer (router mode) or a cancellable scheduled reboot (other modes) so a lockout rolls back automatically, with a visible countdown because the rollback reboots the router for U6 | Reboot-reverts semantics are the owner's safety net; a rollback is an internet outage for the household |
| IS-5 | Migrating old -> new router yields a reviewable plan (copy / remap / skip / needs-input / unsupported / conflict, each with a reason) before the target is touched, then applies, verifies (listing anything left only on the target), and commits or rolls back, ending with a downloadable report; sources can be a live router on **any** firmware (startup-config download), an uploaded startup-config, or a stored backup; the target must run 4.3+ on the same or a newer firmware version than the source config (older targets are refused) and is expected to be clean/factory-fresh (a non-clean target gets a warning listing its leftover settings) | The old router is usually on older firmware; models differ in ports, radios, components; some secrets are model-bound |
| IS-6 | Secrets (router passwords, tokens, PSKs, keys, session ids, API keys) never appear in logs, audit entries, tool results, error messages, `Debug`, or rendered pages unless an authorized user explicitly reveals/exports them, which is audited | Guidelines S10; the startup-config carries reversible `ns3` secrets |
| IS-7 | The Web UI works in en/ru/uk/de/be/kk, light/dark, desktop and phone widths, under a strict CSP with no inline script, and shows live status via SSE | Owner choice; Keenetic markets |
| IS-8 | Every Web/MCP/CLI action is authorized by one permission model (admin/operator/viewer + router scoping) and recorded in a tamper-evident audit log | Shared deployment safety |
| IS-9 | The MCP surface is LLM-usable: the default `core` toolset has <= 25 tools and all opt-in toolsets together <= 70, results are capped at 32 KiB with pagination, annotations are accurate, read-only mode registers no write tools, destructive actions need confirmation plus a server switch, and client identities can be redacted (`redact_clients`) before they reach a third-party model | Context window, agent safety, privacy of household device data |
| IS-10 | A networked deployment is TLS-capable, authenticated, rate-limited and OWASP-hardened; outbound requests go only to registered routers inside allowlisted networks | http-services + mcp-servers overlays |
| IS-11 | The codebase passes the core Section 9 lint profile verbatim, cargo-deny/audit/vet/machete, rustdoc `-D warnings`, the full Section 12 CI list, and ships binaries for linux x86_64/aarch64 (musl), macOS aarch64, windows x86_64 plus an OCI image | Mandatory owner requirement + distribution answer |
| IS-12 | Every behavior is provable without hardware against an in-repo fake router, and env-gated live suites (read + write + migration rehearsal) run against the owner's spare test routers only (serial-number allowlist) | Agent-executable QA without risking a production router |
| IS-13 | kcc never overloads a router: <= 2 RCI requests in flight per router, one shared poller per router (light data >= 15 s, heavy data >= 60 s, never NAT/process tables), sized for 50 routers, 20 web users and 10 MCP sessions; no client telemetry history is stored | Small MIPS routers; `show rc` and NAT tables are expensive; stored presence history is personal data |
| IS-14 | Every registered router and kcc itself are observable through a Prometheus exporter (system, internet checks, interfaces, Wi-Fi, clients, VPN tunnels, mesh, storage, config state, kcc request/auth/job/log-pipeline health); scrapes never trigger router I/O; per-client series are opt-in and capped; Grafana dashboard and alert rules ship with it | Owner request: monitoring/metrics |
| IS-15 | Router system logs are collected through RCI (background stream with snapshot fallback), parsed, deduplicated and forwarded to Loki and to Elasticsearch/OpenSearch (and stdout JSON), with bounded buffering, retries and visible loss counters | Owner request: logs to Loki/Elastic |
| IS-16 | The `kcc` binary refuses to run as root (any UID 0, which covers `sudo`), with Windows elevation, or with capabilities it does not need; it drops held capabilities, sets `no_new_privs`, verifies the result before starting any thread, and refuses listen ports below 1024 - mirroring krb-passwd-ui's privilege floor | Owner request: block sudo / unnecessary privileges |
| IS-17 | The OCI image runs in rootless Podman with a shipped seccomp allowlist, read-only root filesystem, all capabilities dropped and `no-new-privileges`, proven by driving every I/O path (router RCI, TLS, Web UI, MCP, metrics, log sinks, migration) inside the container under the profile | Owner request: rootless Podman + seccomp |
| IS-18 | An operator can capture named presets of settings groups - DoH/DoT + DNS filtering, DNS records + domain routing, connection policies, static routes, firewall rules, VPN client tunnels, Wi-Fi networks, system & services baseline, router users, schedules - from a live router, a backup or a config file, and apply a preset (or copy groups directly, as a one-off preset) to one target router on the same or newer firmware, with preview (merge or mirror), interface remapping, verification and rollback | Owner request: copy DoH/DoT and other settings between routers via presets |

### Must have
- Cargo workspace `crates/*` + separate `xtask/` workspace; edition 2024; `rust-version = "1.99.1"`; no `rust-toolchain.toml`; every crate `publish = false`; license `MIT OR Apache-2.0`.
- Core Section 9 lint profile copied **verbatim** into `[workspace.lints]` and `xtask/Cargo.toml`; `clippy.toml` = Section 9 sample + `doc-valid-idents` proper nouns + documented duplicates; `[build] warnings = "deny"`; `deny.toml` = Section 10 strict sample; zero `#[allow`/`#![allow` in the tree.
- Crates: `kcc-rci`, `kcc-catalog`, `kcc-config`, `kcc-domain`, `kcc-migrate`, `kcc-app`, `kcc-telemetry` (metrics + log pipeline), `kcc-i18n`, `kcc-web`, `kcc-mcp`, `kcc` (binary), `kcc-testkit` (fake router, fake log sinks, fixtures, capture tool).
- Firmware support 4.3 LTS, 5.0, 5.1 and 5.2 (catalog per documented version, fake-router profiles and tests on 4.3, 5.0 and 5.1). Support tiers: `hardware-verified` (firmware lines exercised by the live suites on the owner's 5.1+ routers) and `documentation-based` (4.3 LTS, 5.0 - official manuals + fake router only); the tier is shown in the Web UI, CLI and MCP, and a migration to a documentation-based target needs an explicit, audited acknowledgement.
- Presets: capture named settings-group presets from a router/backup/file and apply them (or copy groups directly) to one target router, one-to-one (todos 58-60).
- Prometheus exporter, Grafana dashboard + alert rules; router log forwarding to Loki and Elasticsearch/OpenSearch; privilege floor in the binary; rootless-Podman image with a seccomp allowlist (todos 54-57).
- Auth: ndw2, ndw4, `X-NDMA-TKN` token, Digest (port-79 proxy), scheme auto-detect with preference ndw4 > ndw2.
- Catalog generated by `cargo xtask catalog` from the official per-model CLI Reference PDFs (5.1 now; 5.2 when published), committed as facts-only data with provenance, plus a hand-maintained `catalog/overlay.toml` (resource kinds the PDF omits, background processes, destructive and self-persisting commands, secret arguments, show paths that need POST); commands outside the catalog (e.g. new in 5.2) are reachable only through an audited admin `unvalidated` escape hatch.
- Web UI: askama SSR + htmx + SSE, vendored pinned assets, strict CSP, local accounts with roles, CSRF, sessions, 6 locales, fleet dashboard, all curated domain pages, command explorer, backups, migration wizard, admin settings, audit viewer.
- MCP via rmcp-server-kit 3.21.x: stdio (opt-in) + Streamable HTTP, API keys + RBAC generated from the permission model, generic + curated + config + migration tools.
- Migration engine with plan/apply/verify/commit/rollback/report, applied via RCI `parse` under `system configuration fail-safe timer`.
- Release: musl static linux x86_64/aarch64, macOS aarch64, windows x86_64, OCI image.
- Wave-1 spikes with numeric pass gates and pre-written fallbacks for every unverified assumption (todos 52, 53; catalog gate in todo 13).
- Capacity and privacy limits from IS-13; `docs/PRIVACY.md` data inventory; README states the project is unofficial and not affiliated with Keenetic/Netcraze.

### Must NOT have (guardrails, anti-slop, scope boundaries)
- No `unsafe` (`unsafe_code = "forbid"`), no `#[allow]`, no manifest-level allows beyond the profile's `redundant_pub_crate`, no `unwrap`/`expect`/`panic!` in non-test code, no `println!`/`eprintln!`/`dbg!`.
- No XML `/ci` API (deprecated), no Keenetic Cloud/RMM integration, no firmware modification, no on-router (Entware) build, no OS keychain integration, no TUI.
- No skipping TLS verification for routers (verify or SHA-256 pin only); no following HTTP redirects to routers.
- No automatic `system configuration save` after a change unless the caller explicitly asks; MCP never saves on its own.
- No raw tool/form input interpolated into RCI paths or CLI lines without catalog validation and identifier validation.
- No npm/Node toolchain in the build; no CDN assets; no inline `<script>`/`style=` attributes; no `hx-on` (needs eval).
- No committing downloaded vendor PDFs or any manual prose (the catalog stores facts only: paths, arguments, types, ranges, flags, since-version, page number, source URL + sha256); no real router secrets/MACs/IPs in fixtures.
- No stored client telemetry/presence history (live charts are in-memory ring buffers only).
- No silent acceptance of a router's user agreement (DPN/EULA): the operator confirms the router's own text, and it is audited.
- No pushes to external repositories (e.g. the rust-guidelines overlay PR) without explicit owner approval; no git commits unless the owner requested them in the execution session.
- No live write tests unless `KCC_LIVE_ALLOW_WRITE=1` and the router profile is marked `test_device = true`.
- No override flag for the privilege floor ("prohibited, no excuses"); no `std::process::Command` in product crates.
- No multi-target or fleet-wide preset apply (owner chose one-to-one), no preset export/import files, no preset editor beyond per-item include/exclude at apply time; no migration or preset apply onto older firmware than the source.
- No syslog listener, no disk spool for log forwarding, no OpenTelemetry/Influx exporters, no Kubernetes manifests (not requested); no per-client metric series unless enabled.
- The metrics listener never binds a non-loopback address by default (it is unauthenticated, R-KIT GUIDE metrics section).

## Verification strategy
> Zero human intervention - all verification is agent-executed.
- Test decision: **tests written in the same todo as the code** (tests-after within the todo); auth/crypto known-answer vectors and parsers are test-first. Frameworks: built-in `#[test]`/`#[tokio::test]`, `proptest` (parsers, validators, codecs, MCP argument fuzzing), `cargo nextest` profiles (`.config/nextest.toml`), the `kcc-testkit` axum fake router for integration, env-gated live suites, Playwright MCP (agent-driven browser) for UI QA in F3.
- Test tiers (core S13): `tests/unit` behaviour lives in inline `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`; integration tests in each crate's `tests/*.rs` use the fake router only; live tests in `crates/kcc/tests/live_*.rs` are `#[ignore]`d and run only via `cargo test -p kcc --test <name> -- --ignored` when `KCC_LIVE_*` env vars are present. Documented in `AGENTS.md`.
- Live env contract: `KCC_LIVE_URL` (e.g. `http://192.168.1.1`), `KCC_LIVE_LOGIN`, `KCC_LIVE_PASSWORD_FILE`, `KCC_LIVE_ALLOW_WRITE=1` (write suites), `KCC_LIVE_SOURCE_FILE` (a saved startup-config used as the migration/preset source - the owner has a single 5.1.x unit, so this is the default rehearsal source) or `KCC_LIVE_SOURCE_URL`/`_LOGIN`/`_PASSWORD_FILE`, and `KCC_LIVE_TARGET_URL`/`_LOGIN`/`_PASSWORD_FILE` (migration and preset rehearsal target). `KCC_LIVE_ALLOW_SERIAL` (comma-separated router serial numbers; every live test reads the serial via `show identification` and refuses any router not listed), `KCC_LIVE_ALLOW_REBOOT=1` (only for probes that may reboot a test router). A missing variable makes the live test return early with a logged skip reason, never pass silently: each live test asserts the skip is reported through `tracing` and returns `Ok(())` only after logging `live-skip:<reason>`.
- **Standard gate G0** (every Rust todo must leave it green; run from the repo root):
  1. `cargo +nightly-2026-10-03 fmt --all -- --check` (dated nightly because `rustfmt.toml` uses `imports_granularity`/`group_imports`, as rmcp-server-kit does)
  2. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`
  3. `cargo test --workspace --all-features`
  4. `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc --workspace --no-deps --all-features`
  5. `cargo xtask allow-gate` (no `#[allow(`/`#![allow(` anywhere) and `cargo xtask profile-eq`
- **Tooling** (installed by todo 1, versions logged): stable >= 1.99.1 (`rustup update stable`), `nightly-2026-10-03` with rustfmt, `cargo-deny`, `cargo-audit`, `cargo-vet`, `cargo-machete`, `cargo-nextest`, `cargo-mutants`, `cargo-zigbuild` + `zig`, `taplo-cli`, `lychee`, `actionlint`, `poppler-utils` (apt; fallback `podman run docker.io/library/debian:12-slim` with poppler-utils installed), `node` (vector generation), Playwright MCP (agent QA), rootless `podman` (>= 4.9) with `crun`, `strace`, `promtool` (Prometheus release binary), `jq`, `shellcheck`. macOS and Windows release legs cannot be built on this Linux host: they are verified only by the first GitHub Actions run after the owner pushes, and reported as unverified until then.
- **Release gate G1** (todo 5 wires it into CI; F2 runs it): G0 + `cargo deny check` + `cargo audit` + `cargo vet` + `cargo machete` + `cargo xtask lint-completeness` + `cargo xtask i18n-check` + `cargo xtask assets-check` + `taplo fmt --check` + per-library default-features clippy/test (`cargo clippy -p <crate> --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p <crate>`) + MSRV `cargo +1.99.1 build --workspace --all-features`.
- Evidence: `.omo/evidence/task-<N>-keenetic-control-center.<ext>`; when executed under ulw-loop, write the same file names into the loop's `currentAttemptDir` instead. (plain logs `.log`, JSON `.json`, screenshots `.png`). Every QA scenario below names its evidence file; the executor captures the exact command and its full output into it (`<cmd> 2>&1 | tee <evidence>`).

  What to do / Must NOT do: In `kcc-rci`: `Credentials { login: String, password: SecretString }` (manual redacted `Debug`, not `Copy`); `WwwAuthenticate` parser for comma-separated multi-scheme headers with quoted params (`x-ndw2-interactive realm=".." challenge=".." session_id=".." session_cookie=".."`, `x-ndw4-interactive realm=".." endpoint=".."`, unknown schemes kept as opaque) using `strip_circumfix` for quoted values (R-GUIDE 2561); ndw2 response = lowercase hex `sha256(challenge || md5hex(login ":" realm ":" password))` with all intermediates in `Zeroizing`; `Session` holding the router-chosen cookie name/value (read from `Set-Cookie`, cross-checked with `session_cookie=`), expiry tracking (TTL from `Max-Age`, refreshed on every response that re-sends the cookie); `SessionManager` with single-flight login (`tokio::sync::Mutex` around login only; requests never hold it across their own `.await` on the wire), `ensure_session()` (GET /auth: 200 -> ok, 401 -> pick scheme -> login), and exactly one transparent re-auth + retry on a 401 for idempotent requests (GET, `show`, `show rc/sc`) and for POSTs only if the 401 came before the body was processed (the router answers 401 without executing); `logout()` (DELETE /auth, best-effort); the session lifetime is treated as unknown (cookie says 300 s, rci-tools prose says 10 min): rely on reactive re-auth plus a keepalive (`GET /auth`) every 120 s while a job or SSE subscriber needs the session. Every async fn carries a `// cancel-safe:` / `// NOT cancel-safe:` comment (R-GUIDE S5). HTTP via `reqwest` 0.13 (`default-features = false`, features `rustls-no-provider` plus an explicit ring provider install consistent with rmcp-server-kit, `json`), redirects disabled, connect/read timeouts configurable (defaults 5 s / 30 s).   What to do / Must NOT do: Implement `x-ndw4-interactive` exactly per R-RCI AUTH.md 98-195 and the TS reference (`ndw4-auth.protocol.ts`, `ndw4-auth.constants.ts`): 16-byte client nonce from `getrandom`/`rand` OS RNG, base64 standard; phase POSTs to `<base><endpoint>`, replies decoded from the `X-NDM-Data` header (base64 JSON), non-empty `error`/`e` -> rejection; validate combined nonce prefix, salt exactly 16 bytes, `iter` 1..=64, `memcost` 8..=262144 KiB, and additionally a local cap `max_argon2_memory_kib` (default 262144, configurable lower) refusing with `AuthError::CostTooHigh`; AuthMessage built from the raw `iter`/`memcost` strings; Argon2id (p=1, 64-byte output, `argon2` 0.6) run in `tokio::task::spawn_blocking` bounded by a process-wide `Semaphore` (default 1 permit, configurable) to cap memory; derived keys (salted password -> client/stored/server keys) cached in `Zeroizing` memory per (router, login, salt, iter, memcost) so re-logins with unchanged parameters skip Argon2; client key = HMAC-SHA3-512(salted, "NDW4 Interactive Client Key"), stored key = SHA3-512(client key), server key = HMAC-SHA3-512(salted, "NDW4 Interactive Server Key"); proof = base64(client key XOR HMAC(stored key, msg)); server signature compared with `subtle::ConstantTimeEq`; mismatch -> `AuthError::ServerSignatureMismatch` without sending phase 3; phase 3 then re-check `GET /auth` == 200. Scheme selection: ndw4 when advertised, else ndw2; config can force one. Token auth (marked `experimental` in config and docs until a 5.2 router passes the live auth matrix of todo 51; based on a single forum source): when a router profile has a token, send `X-NDMA-TKN: <token>` on every request and never call /auth (on 401 -> `AuthError::TokenRejected`, no fallback to password unless the profile also has credentials and `token_fallback = true`). Digest: `DigestProxy` mode for `rci.<name>.keenetic.*`-style KeenDNS names through the HTTP Proxy service: RFC 7616 digest (MD5 and SHA-256, `qop=auth`, nc/cnonce) on each request. Extend `kcc-testkit` with a server-side ndw4 implementation (stores Argon2id salted password per user; test profile uses iter 2, memcost 64 KiB to keep tests fast), token users, and a digest-protected port. Known-answer vector: generate once with the TS reference by running `node scripts/vectors/ndw4-vector.mjs` (script added in this todo; it imports the rci-tools implementation pinned at `1486b62` and `hash-wasm`, fixed nonces/salt) and commit the JSON output to `crates/kcc-rci/tests/vectors/ndw4.json`; the Rust implementation must reproduce every intermediate (salted password, keys, proof, signature). Must NOT fall back from ndw4 to ndw2 after a ndw4 rejection (downgrade); must NOT log nonces, proofs, tokens.

- [x] 10. RCI request engine: verbs, batches, background processes, files, limits, outbound policy
  What to do / Must NOT do: `RciClient` API: `get(path, query)`, `post(path, body)`, `post_root(body)` (always `/rci/` with trailing slash), `delete(path, query)`, `show_rc(tree)`, `show_sc(tree)`, `batch(Vec<Value>)` (splits into ordered chunks below 900 KiB serialized, returns per-item results in input order, maps per-item `error` statuses to per-item `Err` without aborting the others unless `stop_on_error`), `parse(line)` and `parse_batch(lines)` (`{"parse": "<line>"}` items), `show_interface(name)` (always POST `{"show":{"interface":{"name":..}}}` so slash names work), background processes: `start(path, body) -> BackgroundHandle` on a **dedicated session** (own cookie jar; R-RCI RCI_API.md 1488-1502), `poll()` until no `continued`, `stop()` (DELETE), with poll interval 1 s default, overall deadline, and cancellation via `tokio_util::sync::CancellationToken` (stop sent on cancel, cancel-safe documented); file transfer: `download_config(Startup|Running) -> SecretBytes` (`GET /ci/startup-config.txt` / `running-config.txt`), `upload_startup_config(bytes)` (`{"put":{"filename":"flash:startup-config","size":N}}` -> port, then multipart POST `/fui/` with `port` + `Filedata`; marked `#[doc]` as unverified-in-the-field and only callable through migration raw-restore); per-router concurrency `Semaphore` (default 2, IS-13), response body cap (default 8 MiB, `BodyTooLarge`), text responses (e.g. `show/netfilter`) returned as `RciBody::Text`; for `show` resources a GET answered 404 is retried once as POST `{}` (some show commands, e.g. `show log`, `show ipsec`, are POST-only on 5.1 per R-MCP1 docs/rci-api.md 273-281), and the outcome is recorded per router so later calls go straight to POST. Outbound policy `OutboundPolicy` checked before every connect: resolve host, every resolved IP must be inside `allowed_networks` (default 10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 100.64/10, fd00::/8, plus `127.0.0.0/8` and `::1` only when `allow_loopback = true`), and hostnames ending in an allowlisted suffix list (default `.keenetic.pro`, `.keenetic.link`, `.keenetic.name`, `.keenetic.io`, `.keenetic.net`, `.netcraze.pro`, `.netcraze.link`, `.netcraze.io` - the netcraze ones marked UNVERIFIED in docs) may resolve to public IPs; `169.254.169.254`, `fd00:ec2::254`, `100.100.100.200` always refused; pin the resolved IP for the connection (reqwest `resolve`) to prevent DNS rebinding. TLS: `TlsMode::Verify` (webpki roots via rustls) or `TlsMode::PinSha256(fp)` (custom `ServerCertVerifier` comparing the leaf SPKI sha256, still verifying signatures/handshake); never "skip". Must NOT follow redirects; must NOT retry non-idempotent POSTs on transport errors.

  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-rci --test engine 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-10-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - the outbound-refusal and wrong-pin tests, output appended.

`Catalog::lookup(path)`; `Catalog::search(query, limit)` (tokenized, prefix-weighted ranking over path, argument names and the synonym table - no manual prose exists to search); `validate(path, method, body/query) -> Result<ValidatedRequest, Vec<ValidationIssue>>`: path must exist; method allowed by kind (Setting: GET/POST, DELETE only with `prefix_no`; Show: GET/POST; Action: POST; **Background** - from the overlay `background` list or runtime-learned `continued:true` - POST to start, GET to poll and DELETE to stop regardless of `prefix_no`, per R-CLI 30013-30018); every body key must be an argument or a nested sub-command of that command (recursive per R-CLI App. B nesting rule), value types checked (Integer range if known, IPv4/IPv6/mask/MAC formats, Interface names via `ObjectName`, keyword membership), `"no": true` only where `prefix_no`; unknown keys produce `ValidationIssue::UnknownField { suggestion }` using Damerau-Levenshtein <= 2 against siblings; `to_json_schema(path)` (schemars-compatible JSON Schema object used by MCP describe tools and Web forms); runtime-learned flag `background: bool` stored per router when a response carried `continued:true`; `risk(path) -> Risk::{Read, Write, Management, Destructive}` from kind + overlay (`destructive` list, management-affecting paths such as `interface * ip address`, `interface * up/down`, `ip http *`, `user *`); `unvalidated` escape hatch: a request outside the catalog (e.g. a 5.2-only command) is allowed only when the caller holds `RawRci` and `Admin`-level `ManageRouters`, passes `unvalidated: true`, and the request is audited with the flag - it is never used by curated domains or migration. Property tests: `validate` never panics on arbitrary JSON; every catalog entry's generated schema is valid JSON Schema (draft 2020-12 meta-check via `jsonschema` dev-dep or a structural check).

- [x] 15. Config-text AST, flattener, ns3 codec and secret registry (kcc-config)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Parser for KeeneticOS startup/running-config text: header lines `! $$$ <Key>: <Value>` -> `ConfigHeader { model, version, agent, last_change, md5, username, extra }`; other `!` lines are separators/comments; indentation (1 space per level in startup-config, 4 in some exports - detect per file) builds a tree of `Node { tokens: Vec<Token>, children, line_no }`; tokenizer handles double-quoted args with spaces and `\"`/`\\` escapes; `Token::Ns3(String)` for `ns3 <blob>` pairs. Printer emits canonical text (1-space indent, `!` after each top-level block) and `parse(print(ast)) == ast` (proptest over generated ASTs). `flatten(&Ast) -> Vec<CliLine>`: each leaf becomes a full-path single line by prefixing ancestor tokens (`interface Bridge0` + `ip address 192.0.2.1 255.255.255.0` -> `interface Bridge0 ip address 192.0.2.1 255.255.255.0`); a context node with no children becomes its own line; order preserved. `ns3` codec: `decode(&str) -> SecretString` and `encode(&SecretString) -> String` implementing the public algorithm (R-NS3, PowerShell reference); vectors listed in R-NS3 plus encode->decode proptest for ASCII printable passwords of length 1..=63; invalid length -> error. Secret registry: a table of command paths and argument positions that carry secrets (`interface * authentication wpa-psk`, `authentication password`, `iapp key`, `ipsec preshared-key`, `wireguard private-key`, `wireguard peer * preshared-key`, `crypto ike key`, `user * password md5|nt|plain`, `interface * web-api password`, `chilli radiussecret|uamsecret|macpasswd`, `wpa-eap radius secret`, `snmp community`, `ip http proxy * password` if present in catalog) - built from the catalog `secret` flags (argument type `Password` + overlay) and cross-checked against this list; `redact(&Ast) -> Ast` replaces secret tokens with `<redacted>`; normalizer for diffing (sort-insensitive where the router itself reorders: none by default; whitespace + quoting canonicalized). Must NOT decode ns3 anywhere except the explicit reveal/export paths (todo 20/26/41).

- [x] 18. Application config schema and state store (kcc-app)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Root config `kcc.toml` (`#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]` on every struct): `[server]` = `rmcp_server_kit::config::ServerConfig`, `[observability]` = kit `ObservabilityConfig`, `[web]` (`enabled`, `session_idle = "30m"`, `session_absolute = "12h"`, `login_max_failures = 5`, `lockout = "15m"`, `default_locale = "en"`, `public_url`), `[mcp]` (`http_enabled = true`, `stdio_enabled = false`, `read_only = false`, `allow_destructive = false`, `max_result_bytes = 32768`), `[outbound]` (`allowed_networks`, `allowed_suffixes`, `allow_loopback = false`), `[state] dir` (default: `$XDG_STATE_HOME/kcc` / `%LOCALAPPDATA%\kcc` / `~/Library/Application Support/kcc`), `[backups] keep_last = 50`, `max_age = "180d"`, `[audit] retention = "365d"`, `[cli] role = "admin"`, `router_scope = []` (empty = all), `[privacy] redact_clients_in_mcp = false`, `[[routers]]` (config-defined, read-only in the UI: `name`, `url`, `login`, `password_file` | `token_file`, `auth = "auto"|"ndw2"|"ndw4"|"token"|"digest"`, `tls = "verify"|{pin_sha256}`, `test_device = false`, `tags`). Env overrides via kit `apply_env_overrides` for kit sections and `KCC_*` for ours, reported in an audit-style startup report. State store: directory with `0700` (unix) created via `std::fs::DirBuilder` + `PermissionsExt`, files written atomically (write temp in same dir, `fsync`, rename) with `0600`; Windows: owner-only DACL as described below; every state file is versioned JSON `{"schema": N, ...}` with a migration registry (`fn migrate(from) -> to`) so later releases upgrade state in place and refuse newer-than-supported schemas; concurrent processes (`kcc serve`, `kcc mcp stdio` spawned per MCP client, ad-hoc `kcc` CLI runs) share one state dir safely: every read-modify-write of a state file holds an exclusive advisory lock on `<file>.lock` (std `File::lock`, stable since Rust 1.89), per-router write leases live in `<state>/leases/<router>.lock` (used by todo 20), and the audit appender takes the audit lock to read the last hash before appending; a state-schema migration first copies the dir to `<state>/pre-migration-<schema>-<ts>/`. Secret store: router credentials in `routers.json` encrypted with XChaCha20-Poly1305 (`chacha20poly1305` crate) using a 32-byte key from `KCC_MASTER_KEY_FILE` or, by default, the **config** dir (`$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/kcc/master.key`, `%APPDATA%\kcc\master.key`, `~/Library/Application Support/kcc/master.key`) - never inside the state dir, so a copy of the state dir does not carry its own key - created on first run with the OS RNG; on Windows the key and state files get an owner-only protected DACL via the `windows-permissions` crate (the same mechanism rmcp-server-kit uses for its audit log, `Cargo.toml` 182-192), on unix 0600/0700; AAD = router id; nonce random per write. Must NOT store plaintext secrets in state files or logs.
  Closes: GAP-7, GAP-10
  Parallelization: Wave W2 | Blocked by: 1 | Blocks: 19, 20, 33, 48
  References: R-KIT `examples/config_file_server.rs` (root config composition, `deny_unknown_fields`, env overrides), `docs/GUIDE.md` 1150-1300; R-GUIDE S10 (secrets, file times via handles 2635-2677), core checklist "Secrets" items 2954-2964.
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; tests: unknown key in any section -> error naming it; atomic write survives a simulated crash (temp file left behind is ignored and cleaned); state file with `schema` higher than supported -> refusal; encrypted round-trip; wrong key -> decryption error without panicking; on unix the created dir/file modes are 0700/0600; second process lock attempt fails with `StateLocked`.
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-app --test config --test state 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-18-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - config with `[serverr]` table rejected, appended.
  Commit: Y | feat(app): add configuration schema, versioned state store and encrypted secrets
  Recommended task executor category: unspecified-high - config/persistence with security properties

- [x] 19. Permission model, accounts, API keys, audit log
  What to do / Must NOT do: `Permission` enum: `Read`, `Write`, `Save`, `Destructive`, `Migrate`, `RawRci`, `RevealSecrets`, `ManageRouters`, `ManageUsers`, `ViewAudit`. Custom roles in state (`roles.json`) with a router-name glob scope list (same glob semantics as rmcp-server-kit `hosts`). UI accounts (`users.json`): username (1..=64, `[a-z0-9._-]`), Argon2id hash (`argon2` 0.6, OWASP params m=19456 KiB t=2 p=1), role, locale, disabled flag, failure counter + lockout-until, password policy (12..=128 chars, not equal to username). API keys for MCP (`api_keys.json`): created with `rmcp_server_kit::auth::generate_api_key()`, store only the hash + name + role + created/last-used, plaintext shown once; produce `Vec<ApiKeyEntry>`. `ToolSpec { name, permission, annotations }` registry input -> `rbac_config(roles, tools) -> rmcp_server_kit::rbac::RbacConfig` (each role's `allow` = tools whose permission it holds; `hosts` = its router scope). Audit log `audit.jsonl`: one JSON object per line `{ts, seq, actor{kind: web|mcp|cli|system, id}, router, action, target, params_redacted, outcome, request_id, prev_hash, hash}` with `hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical_json(entry_without_hash))`; `verify_chain()`; rotation at 50 MiB keeping the chain across files; writes serialized through one async task (mpsc). Must NOT log plaintext API keys, passwords or secret params.

- [x] 20. Router registry, client pool, poller, backups, change workflow, jobs
  What to do / Must NOT do: `RouterRegistry`: merges config-defined and state-defined profiles (ids are slugs `[a-z0-9-]{1,32}`), CRUD for state profiles (requires `ManageRouters`), `test_connection(profile)` (auth + `RouterInfo`), one shared `RciClient` per profile (lazy, rebuilt on profile change). `Poller`: per router, refreshes a `Snapshot { info, system, internet, interfaces_summary, clients_count, wifi_summary, last_change, fetched_at }` - light items every 15 s, heavy items (`show ip hotspot`, `show interface` full) every 60 s, never `show ip nat` or `show processes` - while it has subscribers (tokio `watch`), backs off to 120 s on errors, stops when idle 2 min; when the profile has an optional read-only router credential (`read_login`/`read_password_file`, router user tagged `readonly`), the poller uses it - all front-ends share it (protects small routers). `BackupStore`: `<state>/backups/<router>/<UTC timestamp>-<reason>.txt` (startup-config text, encrypted at rest with the secret-store key because it contains ns3 secrets), list/get/delete, retention (`keep_last`, `max_age`), diff (via `kcc-config` normalizer). `ChangeSet` workflow used by every write path (domains, explorer, migration, MCP, CLI): (0) acquire the cross-process per-router write lease (todo 18; shown in the UI as "router busy: <actor>"), (1) permission check including router scope (for migration: both source and target), (2) catalog validation, (3) read `show last-change` (checksum/agent/user) and if it changed since the actor's last read by a different agent, return `Conflict` unless `force`, (4) automatic backup before the first change of a change set (per router per 30 min window), (5) apply, (6) read-back verification spec (path + expected values), (7) audit entry, (8) return `unsaved = true`; `save(router)` and fail-safe `commit` first re-check `show last-change` (refuse with `Conflict` if another agent changed the router since the change set began, because save/commit persist everyone's unsaved edits) -> `system configuration save` then poll `show last-change` until `unsaved == false` (timeout 30 s). `JobRunner`: long operations as jobs with id, kind, router, progress events (`tokio::broadcast`), cancellation token, persisted status (`jobs/<id>.json`), at most 1 write job per router at a time; on startup, jobs left `running` are marked `interrupted` and the registry checks each such router for an armed fail-safe timer or scheduled reboot and surfaces it (UI banner, MCP `kcc_config_state`, CLI `config state`) with commit/rollback/cancel actions. Must NOT save automatically; must NOT allow two concurrent write jobs on one router.
  Closes: GAP-5, GAP-13
  Parallelization: Wave W5 | Blocked by: 10, 11, 18, 19 | Blocks: 21, 30, 35, 41, 45
  References: R-MCP1 docs/rci-api.md 212-260 (save is asynchronous; poll `show/last-change`; fail-safe object), `plugins/keenetic/skills/keenetic-safe-changes/SKILL.md` (backup before first change, read back, never auto-save); R-MCP2 safety-and-workflows.md 18-23.
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; fake-router tests: first write triggers exactly one backup; a silent-no-op fault on the written field makes the change set return `VerificationFailed` naming the field; a concurrent change by another agent (fake router `last-change` altered between read and write) returns `Conflict`; save polls until saved; two simultaneous write jobs on one router -> second gets `RouterBusy`; poller makes <= 1 request/15 s per light resource with 10 subscribers, and the per-router lease blocks a second process (spawned `kcc` CLI) from writing while a job holds it.
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-app --test workflow --test poller 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-20-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - the `VerificationFailed` and `Conflict` test outputs appended.
  Commit: Y | feat(app): add router registry, shared poller, backups, verified change workflow and jobs
  Recommended task executor category: deep-low - concurrency + workflow semantics, fully specified

  What to do / Must NOT do: Data-driven rules in `crates/kcc-migrate/rules/rules.toml` (embedded), each `{ id, match = "<command path glob over flattened lines>", disposition = copy|remap|skip-hardware|skip-identity|needs-input|unsupported|conflict, reason_key = "<i18n key>", requires_component = optional, condition = optional }`; evaluation order: explicit rule > catalog check (line's command absent from the target catalog view -> `unsupported`) > component check (`requires_component` not installed on target -> `needs-input: install <component>`) > default `copy`. Each item carries before/after text (secrets redacted) and the rule id. Must NOT silently drop any source line: every flattened line maps to exactly one item.

- [x] 29. Migration: planner, diff and reports
  What to do / Must NOT do: `Planner::plan(source, target_inventory, maps, choices) -> MigrationPlan`: ordered `Vec<PlannedLine>` = classified items with disposition copy/remap (after rename), in source startup-config order (the router's own replay order), minus lines whose normalized form already exists in the target running-config (idempotency); optional prune list (target lines absent in source) only for operator-selected items, emitted as `no ...` lines after the additive lines; management-change detection: lines that change the address/subnet/security-level of the interface the target connection uses, or `ip http port`/`ssl` settings, are moved to a final `management` phase with `reconnect_url` (operator-confirmed new URL); `needs-input` items block planning until resolved (`choices`); plan carries `plan_id`, source/target fingerprints (startup-config md5 + `show last-change` checksum) so apply can detect drift. Clean-target check: before apply, the plan lists target settings that are not factory defaults and not in the source (the same residual computation as below, run against the pre-apply running-config); a non-empty list makes the plan carry a `target_not_clean` warning shown by the wizard, CLI and MCP (not a refusal). Verification spec includes a residual list: normalized target lines absent from the source that the plan does not prune (factory defaults kept on the target), shown in the report. Reports: `MigrationReport` JSON (schema-versioned), Markdown and standalone HTML (askama template, no external assets, secrets redacted) with per-disposition counts and every item's reason (localized via `kcc-i18n`). Dry-run = plan + report without contacting the target beyond inventory. Must NOT include decoded secrets in any report.

- [x] 30. Migration: applier with preflight, fail-safe, verify, commit/rollback, raw restore
  What to do / Must NOT do: `Applier` runs as a todo-20 job: preflight depends on `MigrationSource`: `LiveRouter` - source and target URLs must differ and both be reachable (the wizard explains the common 192.168.1.1 collision and how to reach a factory-fresh target on another address first); `File` and `Backup` - no source connectivity is required or attempted, and a backup may target the router it was taken from (restore mode) even when other routers are offline; for every source kind: a target whose `support_tier()` is `DocumentationBased` requires an operator acknowledgement (`ack_documentation_tier = true` in the plan choices; Web/CLI/MCP all pass it explicitly) that is audited, else preflight stops with `UnverifiedFirmwareTier`; auth - a factory-fresh target that answers `/auth` with 200 and no password gets an admin password set as step 1, then kcc logs in again and updates the stored credential; `system mode` (router -> fail-safe timer, other modes -> scheduled reboot via `SafetyNet`), plan fingerprints match current target else `Drift`; user agreements: `show dpn list`/`show eula list` - if not accepted the operator must confirm the router's own text (`show dpn document`/`show eula document`) in the UI/CLI before `dpn accept`/`eula accept` is sent, audited, never silently; components to install: install + `components commit` + wait for reboot + re-inventory, then re-plan), backup target startup-config, arm `system configuration fail-safe timer reboot <N>` (default 900 s, configurable 300..=3600), apply additive lines via `parse_batch` in chunks of 50 with per-line status capture and a `keep-alive` after each chunk; lines that fail get one deferred retry pass after all other lines (boot-time parsing is more lenient than interactive parsing, R-CLI 1822-1823), and lines still failing become reported failures; if the router rejects `ns3`-encoded secret input (todo 53 result), the line is re-sent with the secret decoded and passed as plaintext through the secret path (never logged or reported); stop policy `StopOnError` (default) or `ContinueOnNonCritical` (only for lines whose rule marks them non-critical), management phase last: realm-bound user password lines (`password plain` with operator-provided secrets) and management address/port changes, then switch the client to `reconnect_url` and the new credential, re-auth within 120 s; verify: fetch running-config, normalize, assert every planned line present and no unplanned difference except lines the router rewrites (normalization table), run `show internet status` and interface link checks; finish: operator chooses `commit` (`system configuration fail-safe commit` then `system configuration save`, poll `show last-change`) or `rollback` (`system configuration fail-safe rollback`, wait for reboot, verify startup-config md5 equals the pre-apply backup); if the job loses the router for longer than the timer, report `RolledBackByRouter` after reconnecting to the original URL. Progress events per line. `RawRestore` mode (same `hw_id` only, explicit flag): upload via `upload_startup_config` + `system reboot` + reconnect + verify md5. Must NOT commit automatically (commit is a separate operator action); must NOT continue after an `error` status under `StopOnError`.
  Closes: GAP-6, GAP-5
  Parallelization: Wave W8 | Blocked by: 16, 20, 29, 53 | Blocks: 31, 43, 46, 47
  References: R-CLI 26955-27052 (fail-safe commit/keep-alive/rollback/timer, timer persists across reboots, router mode only); R-MCP1 docs/rci-api.md 243-260 (fail-safe as connectivity watchdog), 212-220 (save is async); R-FORUM-UPLOAD; R-CLI 2426-2455 (components commit).
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; fake-router e2e: KN-1010 config -> KN-1811 target applies, verifies, commits, and the target startup-config contains every planned line; injected error on line 17 stops the run, rollback restores startup md5; management phase with a changed Bridge0 address reconnects to the new URL; simulated loss of the router past the timer yields `RolledBackByRouter`; raw restore refused when `hw_id` differs; apply from an uploaded startup-config file and from a stored backup both succeed with the original source router stopped (no source requests recorded); factory-fresh target gets its admin password first; unaccepted DPN/EULA blocks apply until the operator confirmation is recorded; a non-router-mode target uses the scheduled-reboot safety net; a 4.3.9 (documentation-based) target stops with `UnverifiedFirmwareTier` without `ack_documentation_tier` and proceeds with it, and the acknowledgement appears in the audit log.
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-migrate --test apply 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-30-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - injected-error stop + rollback output appended.
  Commit: Y | feat(migrate): apply migrations under fail-safe with verification, commit and rollback
  Recommended task executor category: deep-high - multi-phase state machine where correctness is argued from router invariants (timer, reboot, reconnect)

- [x] 33. Web foundation: router mounting, layout, design system, assets, CSP
  What to do / Must NOT do: `kcc-web` exposes `fn web_router(state: WebState) -> axum::Router` to be passed to `McpServerConfig::with_extra_router` (todo 48). **All web routes live under `/ui`** (the kit reserves `/mcp`, `/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/version`, `/admin/status`, `/.well-known/*` and panics on exact overlaps, R-KIT `src/transport.rs` 1185-1200); route strings in todos 34-44 are relative to `/ui`, plus a single `GET /` redirect to `/ui/`. The kit applies its request timeout and body limit only to `/mcp` (R-KIT transport.rs 2748-2756), so the web router adds its own: `TimeoutLayer` 30 s on every route except SSE streams, `RequestBodyLimitLayer` 1 MiB default with explicit larger caps on upload routes, max 4 concurrent SSE streams per session; the kit installs its JSON 404 after merging, so the web router registers its own HTML fallback for `/ui/{*rest}`. Templates with `askama` (latest stable; auto-escaping on; templates in `crates/kcc-web/templates/`), base layout (header with fleet switcher, router status pill, unsaved-changes badge, locale switcher, theme toggle stored in a cookie, nav: Dashboard, Clients, Network, Wi-Fi, Routing, Firewall, DNS, VPN, System, Configuration, Advanced, Migration, Admin), accessible components (forms with labels/errors, tables with sorting via server, dialogs via `<dialog>` + htmx, toasts as an `aria-live` region), CSS in `assets/css/app.css` with design tokens (color, spacing, type scale, radius), light/dark via `prefers-color-scheme` + override class, responsive breakpoints for 360 px and >= 1280 px, focus-visible styles, WCAG AA contrast. Vendored assets in `crates/kcc-web/assets/vendor/`: htmx 2.x (`htmx.min.js`), htmx `sse` extension, uPlot (`uPlot.iife.min.js` + `uPlot.min.css`), each pinned in `assets/ASSETS.lock` (name, version, upstream URL, sha256, license: htmx 0BSD, uPlot MIT) with license texts in `assets/LICENSES/`; served from `/static/<sha256-prefix>/<file>` with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` and `integrity` attributes in templates; `cargo xtask assets-check` verifies hashes and that no template references an un-pinned asset. All app JS in `assets/js/app.js` (no inline script). htmx configured by `<meta name="htmx-config" content='{"allowEval":false,"allowScriptTags":false,"includeIndicatorStyles":false,"selfRequestsOnly":true}'>`. CSP via rmcp-server-kit `SecurityHeadersConfig.content_security_policy` = `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; font-src 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; object-src 'none'` plus `; upgrade-insecure-requests` only when TLS is enabled (the kit default includes it, which would break plain-HTTP loopback use); applies to MCP responses too, which is harmless. Generic error pages (404/403/500, localized, no internals). A test lints every template: no `style=` attributes, no `<script>` without `src`, no `hx-on`, no literal user-visible text outside `{{ t(...) }}` calls (regex check over template text nodes, allowlisting punctuation/numbers). Must NOT load anything from a CDN; must NOT use `unsafe-inline` or `unsafe-eval`.

  References: R-KIT `docs/GUIDE.md` 2087-2170 (extra routes bypass auth, `PeerAddr`, extra-route rate limit), 2693-2770 (security headers); R-GUIDE `overlays/domains/http-services.md` 17-58; htmx docs `https://htmx.org/docs/#config`, `https://htmx.org/extensions/sse/`; uPlot `https://github.com/leeoniya/uPlot`.

- [x] 34. Web authentication, sessions, CSRF, authorization middleware
  What to do / Must NOT do: Server-side in-memory session store (`tokio::sync::RwLock<HashMap<SessionId, Session>>` with a periodic sweep; a restart logs everyone out by design) keyed by 256-bit random ids (OS RNG, base64url); cookie `__Host-kcc_session` (Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict, Path=/) when served over TLS, and `kcc_session` (HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict) only when bound to loopback without TLS - startup refuses a non-loopback plain-HTTP bind for the Web UI unless `public_url` is https behind a trusted proxy configured via kit `trusted_proxies`; idle and absolute timeouts from config; id rotation on login and on privilege change (fixation defense); logout invalidates server-side. Login page + POST `/login`: Argon2id verify in `spawn_blocking`, per-IP rate limit (kit `with_extra_route_rate_limit` covers the whole web router; additionally per-username lockout from todo 19), uniform error message and constant-ish timing (verify a dummy hash for unknown users). CSRF: per-session token rendered into `<meta name="csrf-token">` and sent by `app.js` via `hx-headers`/`X-CSRF-Token` on every non-GET; server checks token (constant-time) + `Origin`/`Sec-Fetch-Site` same-origin; GET handlers never mutate. Authorization middleware: route table maps each route to a `Permission` and optional router scope (path param `:router`), 403 page otherwise; `current user` extractor. First-run: `kcc admin init` (todo 47) creates the first admin; the web shows a "no admin configured" page with the CLI instruction instead of an open setup form. Account page: change own password (requires current password), choose locale. Every auth event audited (todo 19). The kit's Origin check also covers the web routes and rejects any Origin not in `allowed_origins`/`public_url` (R-KIT transport.rs 3040-3055, 5565-5605), so `allowed_origins` is derived automatically from `[web] public_url` and the bind address (`http(s)://127.0.0.1:<port>`, `http(s)://localhost:<port>`, `http(s)://[::1]:<port>` for loopback binds) and startup refuses to run the Web UI when the derived set is empty. Changing a password or role ends that user's other sessions. TLS for non-loopback binds: operator cert/key, or `kcc tls self-signed` (todo 47, `rcgen`) which writes a cert into the config dir and prints its SHA-256 fingerprint. Must NOT put session ids or CSRF tokens in URLs or logs.
  Closes: GAP-8, GAP-7
  Parallelization: Wave W4 | Blocked by: 19, 33 | Blocks: 35, 44, 48
  References: OWASP Session Management and CSRF Prevention cheat sheets; R-KIT `docs/GUIDE.md` 2134-2290 (extra-route rate limit, trusted forwarders); R-GUIDE http-services 111-129 (no internals in errors).
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; router tests: login success sets the expected cookie flags; wrong password 5x locks the account; session id changes after login; POST without CSRF token -> 403; POST with token but cross-site `Origin` -> 403; viewer hitting an operator route -> 403; idle timeout via mock clock -> redirect to login; non-loopback plain-HTTP bind refused at startup.
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-web --test auth 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-34-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - CSRF-missing and cross-origin test outputs appended.
  Commit: Y | feat(web): add login, server-side sessions, CSRF and permission middleware
  Recommended task executor category: deep-low - web security mechanics with exact tests

- [x] 45. MCP core: handler, tool registry, generic tools, safety gates
  What to do / Must NOT do: In `kcc-mcp`: `KccMcp` implementing `rmcp::handler::server::ServerHandler` with `#[tool_router]` (rmcp 3.x macros; `#[expect(clippy::unused_async_trait_impl, reason = "async is mandated by the ServerHandler trait")]` only where needed, per R-GUIDE mcp-servers overlay 26-32). Tool registry builds `ToolSpec { name, permission, annotations{read_only_hint, destructive_hint, idempotent_hint, open_world_hint=false} }` for every tool and feeds todo-19 `rbac_config`. Naming `kcc_<area>_<verb>`; every router-targeting tool takes `host: String` = router profile id (so rmcp-server-kit RBAC host globs apply; `rbac.rs` 1959-2000). Generic tools in this todo: `kcc_routers_list`, `kcc_router_info` (includes `support_tier`), `kcc_catalog_search`, `kcc_catalog_describe` (returns the command schema), `kcc_rci_read` (GET setting/show or `show rc|sc`, validated), `kcc_rci_execute` (POST validated body; RawRci permission; returns statuses + `unsaved`), `kcc_rci_delete`, `kcc_rci_batch`, `kcc_cli_parse` (validated line), `kcc_job_status`, `kcc_job_cancel`. Inputs are typed structs deriving `schemars::JsonSchema` + `serde::Deserialize` with `deny_unknown_fields` and length limits validated before use. Toolsets: every tool belongs to one toolset; `[mcp] toolsets` (default `["core"]`) selects which are registered; `core` (<= 25 tools) = the generic tools of this todo plus `kcc_config_state`, `kcc_config_save` and the read tools of system, interfaces, wifi, clients, routing (todo 46); opt-in toolsets: `network-write`, `vpn`, `dns`, `system-admin`, `migration`, `raw` (the `kcc_rci_execute|delete|batch` and `kcc_cli_parse` tools live in `raw`, not `core`). Authorization is decided inside every handler by `kcc-app` permissions (kit RBAC is an outer coarse gate: it checks only one `host` argument and nothing under stdio, R-KIT rbac.rs 1968-1993): tools touching two routers (migration) check both, fleet listings are filtered by the role's router globs. `[privacy] redact_clients_in_mcp = true` replaces client MACs/hostnames/IPs with stable per-session pseudonyms in MCP results. If todo 52 showed `#[tool_router]`/`#[tool]` output cannot pass the lint profile with narrow `#[expect]`s, implement the pre-written fallback: a hand-written `ServerHandler::list_tools`/`call_tool` dispatch over the `ToolSpec` registry with `schemars` input schemas. Result shaping: JSON text content capped at `[mcp] max_result_bytes` (default 32 KiB) with `truncated: true`, `next_cursor` pagination for list-like results, optional `fields` projection, secrets always redacted. Errors on the wire are generic (`invalid_arguments`, `forbidden`, `router_unreachable`, `router_rejected`, `validation_failed`, `conflict`, `internal`) with a `request_id`; details go to `tracing` (R-GUIDE mcp-servers 7-14). Read-only mode (`[mcp] read_only = true`) registers only read tools (write tools absent from `tools/list` and from the router). Destructive tools additionally require `confirm: true` in arguments AND `[mcp] allow_destructive = true`. Resources: `kcc://catalog/<version>/<path>` (command facts), `kcc://router/<id>/snapshot` (poller snapshot); the kit's RBAC does not cover resources, so `resources/list` is filtered and `resources/read` enforced by the handler with `Read` + the identity's router globs (same helper as tools). Prompts: `troubleshoot_internet`, `troubleshoot_wifi`, `troubleshoot_device`, `safe_change_workflow` (texts from i18n `en`, adapted from R-MCP1 skills). Every write goes through todo-20 `ChangeSet` with actor `mcp:<identity from rmcp_server_kit::rbac::current_identity()>`. Must NOT expose a tool that saves implicitly; must NOT exceed 25 tools in `core` or 70 tools across all toolsets.
  Closes: GAP-9
  Parallelization: Wave W6 | Blocked by: 14, 19, 20, 52 | Blocks: 46, 48
  References: R-KIT README 31-98, `docs/GUIDE.md` 1078-1130 (RBAC-filtered tools/list), `src/tool_hooks.rs` (`ToolHooks::with_max_result_bytes`), `src/rbac.rs` 1959-2000; rmcp-macros 3.4 `src/tool.rs` 155-242 (annotations attribute); R-GUIDE `overlays/domains/mcp-servers.md`; R-MCP1 README "Safety" + `src/tools/registry.ts`, `src/shape/budget.ts`; R-MCP2 README (confirm/dryRun/ALLOW_DESTRUCTIVE).

  What to do / Must NOT do: In `kcc-telemetry` (module `metrics`): enable rmcp-server-kit feature `metrics` (adds `prometheus` 0.14; record its duplicates per todo 2) and register a custom `prometheus::core::Collector` on the kit registry via `McpServerConfig::with_metrics(bind)` + `with_metrics_handle(Arc<McpMetrics>)` (wired into `kcc serve` here). Config `[metrics] enabled = false`, `bind = "127.0.0.1:9090"` (the kit listener is plaintext and unauthenticated - non-loopback binds require `allow_non_loopback = true` and log a warning; ports < 1024 refused by todo 56), `interval = "30s"` (min 15 s), `heavy_interval = "120s"`, `per_client = false`, `per_client_max = 256`, `per_client_label = "name"|"pseudonym"|"mac"` (default `pseudonym`). Metrics add no poller of their own: the exporter registers a permanent metrics subscription with the todo-20 shared `Poller` (the only component that issues periodic router reads, IS-13), which then keeps refreshing the light items every `interval` (`show system`, `show internet status`, `show interface` summary, `show interface stat` per up interface, `show last-change`) and the heavy items every `heavy_interval` (`show ip hotspot`, `show media`, `show mws member`, cached components update flag) even without UI subscribers, within the IS-13 in-flight cap; `show ip nat` and `show processes` are never polled. **Scrapes never call a router**: the collector renders the cache; data older than 3x its interval is omitted and `keenetic_up` becomes 0. Metric families (labels always include `router` = profile id): `keenetic_up`, `keenetic_poll_duration_seconds`, `keenetic_info{model,hw_id,brand,firmware,region}` (=1), `keenetic_cpu_load_ratio`, `keenetic_memory_{total,free,cache,buffers}_bytes`, `keenetic_swap_{total,free}_bytes`, `keenetic_uptime_seconds`, `keenetic_conntrack_entries`, `keenetic_conntrack_max`, `keenetic_internet_reachable{check}` (internet/gateway/dns/host/captive), `keenetic_interface_up{interface,type,description}`, `keenetic_interface_link_up`, `keenetic_interface_{receive,transmit}_bytes_total`, `keenetic_interface_{receive,transmit}_packets_total`, `keenetic_interface_{receive,transmit}_errors_total`, `keenetic_interface_uptime_seconds`, `keenetic_radio_temperature_celsius{interface}` (WifiMaster temperature when reported), `keenetic_wifi_clients{access_point,band}`, `keenetic_clients{state="online"|"offline"}`, `keenetic_dhcp_leases`, `keenetic_wireguard_peer_last_handshake_seconds{interface,peer}` (peer = first 8 chars of the public key) when reported, `keenetic_mesh_member_up{member}`, `keenetic_storage_{total,free}_bytes{label}`, `keenetic_firmware_update_available`, `keenetic_config_unsaved`, `keenetic_failsafe_armed`; opt-in per-client `keenetic_client_{receive,transmit}_bytes_total`, `keenetic_client_signal_dbm`, `keenetic_client_link_speed_mbps` capped at `per_client_max` clients per router (overflow counted in `kcc_metrics_series_dropped_total`); kcc self-metrics `kcc_build_info`, `kcc_router_requests_total{router,outcome}`, `kcc_router_request_duration_seconds` (histogram), `kcc_router_auth_total{router,scheme,outcome}`, `kcc_poller_errors_total{router}`, `kcc_jobs{kind,state}`, `kcc_web_sessions`, plus the log-pipeline metrics of todo 55. Ship `deploy/monitoring/grafana-dashboard.json` (Prometheus + Loki datasource variables, router selector; fleet overview, per-router system, interfaces, Wi-Fi, clients, VPN, logs panel) and `deploy/monitoring/prometheus-alerts.yaml` (router down 5m, internet unreachable 5m, CPU > 90% 15m, memory free < 10% 15m, conntrack > 90%, interface error rate, fail-safe armed > 15m, config unsaved > 24h, log sink dropping) with `deploy/monitoring/prometheus-alerts.test.yaml` promtool unit tests. `cargo xtask gen-metric-docs` writes the metric catalogue for todo 50. - [x] 55. Router log collection and forwarding to Loki and Elasticsearch/OpenSearch
  What to do / Must NOT do: In `kcc-telemetry` (module `logs`): `LogCollector` per router when `[logs] enabled = true` (default false) and the router is listed in `[logs] routers` (default all): mode `stream` - on a dedicated RCI session (todo 10 background handle) POST `show log` `{"once": false, "max-lines": 200}`, then GET poll every 5 s (each answer carries `continued: true` and only new records), DELETE on shutdown, restart after session loss/404; mode `poll` fallback (used automatically when stream start fails twice, or forced by config) - POST `{"once": true, "max-lines": 200}` every 30 s. Records are keyed by their numeric id (`{"log": {"<id>": {...}}}`, shape confirmed by todo 53 probe 6 / todo 17 fixture); dedupe with a per-router sliding window of the last 10 000 ids; an id lower than the window minimum after a gap means the buffer was cleared or the router rebooted and is accepted. Parse into `LogRecord { router, id, ts, level, label, process, pid, component, message }`: level from `level`/`label` (I/W/E/C/D); timestamp from the record's time field when present, else from the `[Mon DD HH:MM:SS]` prefix interpreted in the router's timezone (`show clock date`, cached 1 h) with year inference (a time more than 1 day in the future belongs to the previous year); `process[pid]:` and `Component::Sub:` prefixes split out; continuation lines merged. Sinks `[[logs.sinks]]`: `kind = "loki"` (POST `<url>/loki/api/v1/push` JSON `{"streams":[{"stream":{"job":"keenetic","router":..,"level":..},"values":[["<unix ns>","<line>",{"process":..,"component":..,"id":..}]]}]}` - labels limited to job/router/level, other fields as structured metadata; optional `tenant` -> `X-Scope-OrgID`; `basic_auth_user` + `password_file` or `bearer_token_file`; gzip body when `compress = true`); `kind = "elasticsearch"` with `flavor = "elasticsearch"|"opensearch"` (POST `<url>/_bulk` NDJSON, action `{"create":{"_index":<target>}}`, default target data stream `logs-keenetic.syslog-default` for Elasticsearch 8+ (it matches Elasticsearch's built-in data-stream-enabled `logs-*-*` index template; kcc never creates templates, and when an operator disabled the built-in templates, `docs/MONITORING.md` tells them to install one or to set `target` to a plain index), index pattern `keenetic-logs-%Y.%m.%d` for OpenSearch; ECS document `@timestamp`, `message`, `log.level`, `process.name`, `process.pid`, `event.dataset = "keenetic.syslog"`, `event.module = "keenetic"`, `observer.vendor`, `observer.product`, `observer.version`, `host.name`, `labels.router`, `labels.component`, `labels.log_id`; auth `api_key_file` -> `Authorization: ApiKey` or basic; the bulk response's per-item errors are inspected - 429/5xx items retried, other 4xx items dropped and counted); `kind = "stdout"` (JSON lines for container log collectors). Pipeline per sink: bounded queue (`queue_max = 10000`, drop-oldest), batches of <= 1000 records / 1 MiB / 2 s, exponential backoff with jitter capped at 60 s honouring `Retry-After`, flush on shutdown with a 10 s deadline; delivery is at-least-once within the process lifetime and records still queued at exit or dropped on overflow are lost and counted (no disk spool - documented). Metrics (registered with todo 54): `kcc_log_collector_up{router}`, `kcc_log_records_collected_total{router}`, `kcc_log_records_sent_total{sink}`, `kcc_log_records_dropped_total{sink,reason}`, `kcc_log_batches_failed_total{sink,status}`, `kcc_log_queue_depth{sink}`, `kcc_log_lag_seconds{sink}`. Security: sink URLs come only from `kcc.toml`, TLS verification with optional `ca_file`, no redirects, credentials as `SecretString` loaded from files and never logged/exposed; optional `redact_macs = false` replaces MAC addresses in messages with stable pseudonyms (documented in PRIVACY.md). Integration: when the collector runs for a router, the todo-40 log page and the todo-21 log reader subscribe to it instead of opening a second `show log` stream (one stream per router - concurrent same-type background processes collide, R-RCI RCI_API.md 1488-1502). Extend `kcc-testkit`: fake router `show log` stream/once semantics with injectable records, and `FakeLoki`/`FakeElasticsearch` axum servers recording payloads with fault injection (429 + Retry-After, 500, per-item bulk errors, slow responses). Wired into `kcc serve` here. Must NOT open more than one `show log` stream per router or poll faster than every 5 s.
  Closes: GAP-16
  Parallelization: Wave W9 | Blocked by: 10, 20, 21, 48, 54 | Blocks: 50, 51, 57
  References: CLI `show log [max-lines] [once]` (R-CLI section 3.170.81, "executes in the background"); `https://forum.keenetic.ru/topic/10914-` (POST starts, GET returns `continued` + new messages, DELETE stops, session-bound) and `https://forum.keenetic.ru/topic/14215-` (`{"log": {"7297": {"message": {"level": "Info", "label": "I", "message": ...}}}}`, `once`/`max-lines`); R-RCI RCI_API.md 1488-1502; Loki push API `https://grafana.com/docs/loki/latest/reference/loki-http-api/#ingest-logs`; Elasticsearch bulk API and data streams `https://www.elastic.co/docs/api/doc/elasticsearch/operation/operation-bulk`, ECS `https://www.elastic.co/guide/en/ecs/current/index.html`; OpenSearch bulk `https://opensearch.org/docs/latest/api-reference/document-apis/bulk/`.
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; tests: Loki payload matches the push schema (ns timestamps as strings, values sorted per stream, only job/router/level labels); Elasticsearch NDJSON alternates action/document lines, uses `create`, carries the ECS fields; OpenSearch flavor uses the dated index; 429 with `Retry-After: 2` is retried after >= 2 s (mock clock); a sink down for longer than the queue holds drops oldest and increments `kcc_log_records_dropped_total{reason="overflow"}`; stream restart after a simulated session loss produces no duplicate ids; year rollover (router clock Jan 1, record `Dec 31`) yields the previous year; multiline record merged; the sink password never appears in logs or `Debug`; with the collector enabled the web log page opens no second stream (fake router counts streams == 1).
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-telemetry --test logs 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-55-keenetic-control-center.log` plus a manual run of `kcc serve` with fake router + `FakeLoki` showing records arriving; failure - FakeElasticsearch answering per-item `mapper_parsing_exception` drops and counts those items without blocking the rest, appended.
  Commit: Y | feat(telemetry): collect router logs and forward them to Loki and Elasticsearch

- [x] 56. Privilege floor: refuse root/sudo/elevation, drop capabilities, set no_new_privs
  What to do / Must NOT do: `crates/kcc/src/privileges.rs` mirroring krb-passwd-ui `crates/portal/src/privileges.rs` @ `43f6697` (`https://github.com/andrico21/krb-passwd-ui`): Linux - read and strictly parse `/proc/self/status` (`Uid` exactly 4 decimal fields, `CapInh/CapPrm/CapEff/CapBnd/CapAmb` exactly 16 hex digits, `NoNewPrivs` exactly `0|1`, `Threads`; any missing/unparsable field refuses start), refuse if any UID is 0 (covers `sudo` and setuid-root launches), clear only the capability sets that hold bits in the order Ambient, Bounding, Inheritable, Effective, Permitted, set `PR_SET_NO_NEW_PRIVS`, re-read and verify (`CapPrm/CapEff/CapInh/CapAmb == 0`, `CapBnd == 0` only when the bounding drop succeeded - an ordinary host cannot drop it without `CAP_SETPCAP`, `NoNewPrivs == 1`, `Threads == 1`); typed `#[non_exhaustive] PrivilegeError` with the same variants; a `Report` returned for logging after tracing starts. macOS - refuse when the real or effective UID is 0. Windows - refuse when the process token is elevated (an `is_elevated`-style safe wrapper crate, latest stable, recorded in deny/vet). No override flag on any platform. `main` (todo 47) calls `privileges::enforce()` as its first statement, exits with code 77 (`EX_NOPERM`) and a localized one-line reason on refusal, and only then builds the Tokio runtime; this applies to every subcommand (CLI, `serve`, `mcp stdio`, `healthcheck`). Config validation (todo 18 schema) refuses any listen port < 1024 for the main and metrics listeners. Syscall access via `rustix` (already in the graph through rmcp-server-kit: `thread::set_no_new_privs`, `process::getuid/geteuid`) and `caps` 0.5 for capability-set clearing as krb-passwd-ui does (no `nix`). Source guard test `crates/kcc/tests/source_guards.rs`: `privileges::enforce()` is the first statement of `main`; no `std::process::Command`, `Command::new` or `exec(` in any `crates/*/src` (xtask and spikes excluded) - so the seccomp profile never needs a second `execve`. Must NOT build the runtime or open any file before the check.

  References: `/tmp/opencode/research/krb-passwd-ui/crates/portal/src/privileges.rs` 1-400 (enforce, clear order, verify, parse_status, fixtures), `docs/startup-privilege-drop-plan.md` 1-60 (owner requirements: root prohibited, ports < 1024 rejected, `no_new_privs` mandatory; measured table of bare host / podman / `--cap-drop=ALL` states), `crates/portal/src/main.rs` 2558-2600 (prologue before runtime), `crates/portal/tests/source_guards.rs` (~1013, spawner guard).

Operations: `capture(source, groups, name)`, `list`, `get` (secrets redacted unless `RevealSecrets`), `delete`, `plan(preset, target, mode: Merge | Mirror, maps, choices) -> PresetPlan` (Merge = add/update the preset's lines; Mirror = additionally `no` the target's lines in the same groups that the preset lacks; interface/radio/policy name references remapped with the todo-27 map proposals; target-catalog validation per line -> `unsupported` items; firmware rule: target firmware >= preset source firmware else `TargetFirmwareOlder`; documentation-tier target needs `ack_documentation_tier`; `router-users` passwords whose realm differs -> `needs-input` with operator-provided secrets as in migration; `vpn-clients` WireGuard tunnels get `wireguard private-key` **regenerated on the target by default** (`regenerate_wireguard_keys = true`; when false the copied key is used and the plan warns that two routers sharing one key break the tunnel) and the report lists each new public key the operator must register on the VPN server), `apply(plan)` via the todo-30 `Applier` (backup, SafetyNet, `parse_batch`, verify, finish with keep-unsaved / save / rollback - never auto-save) as a todo-20 job with progress. A direct copy = `capture` of a transient (unsaved) preset from the source followed by `plan`/`apply` on one target. Permissions: list/get = `Read`, capture/delete/plan/apply = `Migrate`, both routers scope-checked with `authorize_routers` (todo 45). Every capture/apply/delete is audited. Secrets at rest: every persisted object that contains config lines or operator-supplied secrets - saved and transient presets, preset and migration plans, job records, resumable wizard state (todos 43/59, `<state>/migrations/<id>/`, `<state>/presets/`, `<state>/jobs/`) - is written with the todo-18 authenticated encryption and 0600/owner-only permissions; transient presets live only in memory plus such an encrypted file and are deleted when the run finishes or after 24 h. Scoping: every handler (Web, MCP, CLI) first resolves the preset/transient/backup, authorizes its recorded **source** router with `authorize_routers` before returning metadata, planning or deleting, filters `list` results by the caller's source scope, and separately authorizes the **target**. WireGuard regeneration uses router-side generation (`interface <wg> wireguard private-key` without argument), reads back only the public key from `show interface`, never holds the new private key in kcc, and zeroizes the copied private key material it did not apply.
