- [x] 65. Encrypt router passwords stored by kcc (found by F1 on todo 18)
  What to do / Must NOT do: Todo 18 requires router credentials in the state store to be encrypted (XChaCha20-Poly1305 `MasterKey`, AAD = router id, key outside the state dir) and the plan decision says "Router passwords are stored encrypted in the app's state folder". The tree stores passwords typed into the Web UI (`crates/kcc-web/src/routers.rs` -> `crates/kcc-web/src/credentials.rs`) and the CLI (`crates/kcc/src/cli/routers.rs` `router add/edit --new-password`, KCC_PASSWORD or prompt) as PLAINTEXT 0600 files `<state>/router-credentials/<id>.password`; `README.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md` document this as a limitation. Fix: kcc-managed router passwords become sealed records `<state>/router-credentials/<id>.sealed` (versioned JSON `{"schema":1,"sealed":...}` via `kcc_app::secrets::MasterKey::seal(owner = router id)`, written atomically 0600 under the existing state lock), created/updated/removed by the Web UI and CLI; the registry/`RciClient` credential loading (find where `password_file` is read: `crates/kcc-app/src/registry.rs` and the server assembly in `crates/kcc/src/server.rs`, `credentials_dir`) opens the sealed record for kcc-managed routers and keeps reading operator-supplied `password_file`/`token_file` paths (config-defined routers, files the operator owns) exactly as before; a wrong key or tampered record fails with a typed error without panicking and without echoing secrets; MIGRATION of existing installs: on startup (and on first CLI use) any legacy plaintext `<id>.password` is sealed, verified by opening it, then securely removed (best-effort overwrite then unlink), audited by id only, idempotent, crash-safe (seal first, delete after verification); deleting a router removes its sealed record. Plaintext passwords must exist only in `SecretString`/zeroizing memory. Update `README.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/CONFIG.md`, `docs/PRIVACY.md` (remove the "aren't encrypted" limitation, describe the sealed store, the master key location and that `password_file` paths stay operator-managed) and `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` if it mentions it. Todo 18 also requires, on Windows, an owner-only protected DACL on `master.key` and every state file via the `windows-permissions` crate (the mechanism rmcp-server-kit uses): add it behind `#[cfg(windows)]` with a unit-test seam that does not need Windows where possible, record the crate in deny/vet per Todo-2 rules, and state in the notepad and SECURITY.md that the Windows ACL path is UNVERIFIED on this Linux host. Must NOT change the on-disk format of unrelated state, weaken file modes, log or audit a password, or break routers configured with `password_file`.

  References: todo 18 text; `crates/kcc-app/src/secrets.rs` (`MasterKey`, `SealedSecret`, `master_key_path`), `crates/kcc-app/src/persist.rs` (atomic writes, locks), F1 report `.omo/evidence/F1-keenetic-control-center.md` row 18.
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; tests: a router added through the Web UI and through the CLI leaves NO plaintext password anywhere in the state dir (recursive byte scan for the run-time-built dummy password, including audit, backups, jobs, logs) and the router still authenticates against the fake router; legacy `<id>.password` is migrated (sealed, verified, removed) and the router keeps working; a tampered `.sealed` or a wrong master key yields a typed error and no panic; `password_file` routers behave unchanged; router delete removes the sealed record; the workspace secret scanner stays green.

- [x] 68. RCI detection through the policy-checked pinned client and a shared in-flight budget (found by F4 IS-10/IS-13 and F2 H1)
  What to do / Must NOT do: (a) `RouterInfo::detect` (`crates/kcc-rci/src/capabilities.rs` ~367, `probe_auth_schemes` ~513-530) builds a bare `reqwest::Client` that ignores the router's `TlsMode` pin and the `OutboundPolicy` (no allowed_networks/metadata check, DNS resolved a second time without resolve-once pinning): detection FAILS for every `tls = pin-sha256` router (reproduced: pinned `SessionManager` succeeds, `detect` returns `RciError::Transport`) and the probe is an SSRF/rebinding hole. Run the scheme probe through the session's own policy-checked, pinned client (same `TlsMode`, resolved-once addresses, allowed networks, metadata refusal) and give `RouterInfo::detect` no path around it; keep plain-HTTP behaviour. (b) "<= 2 RCI requests in flight per router" is only a per-`RciClient` `Semaphore`: `RouterRegistry::poll_client` (`crates/kcc-app/src/registry.rs` ~533-541) builds an independent client with its own semaphore when the profile has `read_login`, and `RciClient::start` / `BackgroundHandle` (`engine.rs` ~487-500, background processes) opens its own session without the semaphore, so F4 measured 4 concurrent requests on one router (832 ms for 4 requests at 800 ms latency). Share ONE per-router budget across `registry.client()`, `poll_client()` and background start/poll/stop requests (e.g. an `Arc<Semaphore>` owned per router in the registry and injected into every client built for it). Tests: detection over SPKI-pinned self-signed TLS succeeds with the right pin and fails with a wrong pin; a probe to a non-allowed or metadata address is refused and nothing is sent; a name whose resolution changes after the policy check is not re-resolved; concurrency measured IN THE FAKE ROUTER (max concurrent handlers, not wall-clock): 2 front-end + 2 poller + 1 background request never exceed 2 concurrent at the router; red-first for both. Must NOT loosen TLS (min 1.2), the outbound policy, or the cap.
  Closes: GAP-14

- [x] 82. A failed or cancelled job start must not keep the router's write-job slot (found while annotating row 79)
  What to do / Must NOT do: `crates/kcc-app/src/jobs.rs` `JobRunner::start` (~line 386) reserves the per-router write-job slot (`write_jobs.insert(router, id)`, ~411) BEFORE the fallible/awaiting steps `self.ownership.claim(&id).await?` (~417, returns early on a state-lock or I/O error WITHOUT removing the slot), `self.active.lock().await.insert(..)` and `persist` (the persist-failure branch already cleans up). Any early return or a cancelled `start` future after the slot insert leaves the router's write slot taken for the life of the process, so every later write job on that router answers `JobError::RouterBusy` until restart. Make the reservation exception- and cancellation-safe: either reserve the slot last, or hold it in an RAII guard whose `Drop` releases it (the map is a `tokio::sync::Mutex`: use `try_lock`/a std mutex or a release queue so `Drop` never blocks) that is disarmed only once the job is persisted and spawned; the same for the `ownership` claim and the `active` entry. Tests (red first, deterministic, no sleeps): (a) `claim` failure: pre-hold the job's state lock (look at `jobs/ownership` and `StateStore::try_lock`; the id is `job-<ts>-<seq>`, derive it from the injected clock or make the failing lock deterministic through the existing test hooks, adding a small `#[cfg(test)]`-free seam only if unavoidable) and show a second write job on the same router starts afterwards; (b) a dropped/cancelled `start` future (poll it once past the slot insert with `futures`/`tokio::select!` on a pending branch or `tokio::time::pause`-free manual polling, then drop it) leaves the slot free; (c) the existing job tests stay green. Fix the doc comment: after the fix `start` is cancel-safe up to the spawn (update its `// NOT cancel-safe:` line to the truth). Must NOT change job records, events, recovery behaviour or the on-disk format.

  What to do / Must NOT do: (1) `crates/kcc-rci/src/outbound.rs::allows` canonicalizes only IPv4-mapped (`::ffff:0:0/96`); the suffix path therefore admits forms that EMBED a forbidden IPv4 address: NAT64 well-known `64:ff9b::/96` (`64:ff9b::a9fe:a9fe` = 169.254.169.254), deprecated IPv4-compatible `::/96` (`::a9fe:a9fe`), 6to4 `2002::/16` (`2002:a9fe:a9fe::1`). Extract the embedded IPv4 address for these prefixes (RFC 6052 /96 for 64:ff9b::/96, low 32 bits for `::/96`, bits 16-47 for 2002::/16) and run EVERY check (metadata, unspecified, loopback, private/non-global, `allowed_networks` membership) on the embedded address as well as on the outer one; refuse the local-use NAT64 prefix `64:ff9b:1::/48` and Teredo `2001::/32` outright (never admissible, no setting). Red first: a table test over the forms above with embedded 169.254.169.254, 127.0.0.1, 10.x, 0.0.0.0 and a public address (embedded public address through a suffix host stays admitted only where the outer address is itself global), reproduction in `.omo/evidence/F4-keenetic-control-center-runs-r3/repros.log` (INFO rows). (2) F4 O3: addresses that NO setting can admit (unspecified, multicast, broadcast, cloud metadata, the new embedded forms) get a refusal text saying so (reuse or extend the existing `errors-outbound-metadata` family with a new key `errors-outbound-never` in all six locales: "kcc never connects to {address}: no setting admits this kind of address"), in CLI (`crates/kcc/src/cli/outbound.rs`) and Web (`crates/kcc-web/src/router_check.rs`), instead of the `allowed_networks` hint; add the Fluent variable to `all_arguments` in `crates/kcc-i18n/src/bundle.rs` tests if new; tests in `crates/kcc/tests/cli/outbound.rs` and `crates/kcc-web/tests/dashboard.rs` (en + one non-Latin locale); keep the loopback and general texts as they are. Must NOT weaken deny-by-default or any existing outbound test; keep RFC1918 literals out of test sources (build them with `format!` at run time: the repository anonymizer flags them); update `docs/CONFIG.md` (`[outbound]` section) with one sentence on embedded-address handling.

  What to do / Must NOT do: IS-8 and `docs/SECURITY.md:122` say every change, refusal, login and secret reveal appends an audit entry; Web (`page_kit` `abandon`/`forbidden`, e.g. `web.system_service forbidden`) and CLI (`cli.change failed`, row 76) do, MCP does not. Repro from F4 (`.omo/evidence/F4-keenetic-control-center-runs-r3/is8-mcp-unaudited.log`): (a) a viewer API key calling `kcc_config_save` gets HTTP 403 "denied for role 'viewer'" and the audit line count stays the same; (b) on the silent-no-op fake router the operator key's `kcc_services_write` returns `verification_failed` and nothing is audited, while the CLI appends `cli.change failed` and the Web `web.system_service verification_failed`. (1) Audit, as the MCP actor (`Actor::new(ActorKind::Mcp, key_name)`, never a secret, parameter NAMES and non-secret values only like the other surfaces): every failed change attempt on the MCP write paths (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/domains/write/common.rs` apply paths, `support/errors.rs::map_workflow`, generic `kcc_rci_execute/delete/batch`, config save/commit/rollback, presets/migration tools) with outcome `failed` plus the error kind (`verification_failed`, `router_rejected`, ...), and every refusal that reaches a kcc handler (scope, destructive gate `destructive_disabled`/`confirm_required`, missing permission, validation) with outcome `forbidden` or `refused` plus the reason code, matching the vocabulary the Web/CLI surfaces use. (2) SCOPE DECISION (orchestrator, after the first attempt proved it): the RBAC refusal for a tool the role lacks happens inside rmcp-server-kit 3.21.1 (private `rbac_middleware`, `rbac.rs` ~1722) before any kcc handler runs; the kit has no denial callback, its `tool_hooks` run after RBAC, `with_extra_router` bypasses auth/RBAC, and kcc initialises tracing through the kit (`init_tracing_from_config_strict`), so a bridge would need a new production tracing setup or an upstream change. Neither is done here (no new dependency, no weakening of the kit's RBAC, no 403 -> 200 behaviour change). Instead: (2a) document the limit precisely in `docs/SECURITY.md` (what `audit.jsonl` records for MCP: successful changes, failed changes, and every refusal that reaches a kcc handler; transport-level role denials are refused with HTTP 403 and logged by the kit at warn level with user, role and tool, which the default log filter keeps, but they are NOT appended to the hash-chained audit) and fix the sentence at ~line 122 so it does not overclaim; (2b) record the limit and the two ways to close it (upstream observer hook in rmcp-server-kit, or replacing the kit's tracing init with a kcc layer that bridges the denial event) in the notepad `issues.md` and as an open limitation for the owner. (3) F4 O1: a raw change the router persists at once (`persists_immediately`, e.g. `system country`) is audited as `applied_unsaved` (`router.change_batch ... applied_unsaved`): record a truthful outcome word (for example `applied_persisted`) for such changes on all surfaces that write the audit outcome from `ChangeSet`/the raw RCI paths, keeping the existing word for ordinary changes; update `docs/` where outcome words are listed. Tests (red first, as the MCP actor, through the existing `crates/kcc-mcp/tests/support/lab.rs` harness and the fake router; the silent-no-op fault is `FaultInjection::silent_noop_path`): a refusal that reaches a kcc handler (an operator key calling a tool for a router outside its scope, or a tool refused by the destructive gate) appends exactly one `forbidden`/`refused` entry naming the key and the tool, operator `kcc_services_write` on the no-op router appends `failed`/`verification_failed`, destructive-gate refusals append their reason, a persisted-immediately raw change appends the new outcome word, `kcc audit verify` stays valid, no secret/parameter VALUE of a secret field appears. Must NOT change the audit entry format or hash construction, must NOT audit successful reads, must NOT block the request path on I/O.

- [x] 92. MCP raw RCI tools must send exactly the command they validated (found by F2 round 7 N20 HIGH, N21, N23, N24, N25)
  What to do / Must NOT do: (1) F2 N20 (HIGH, security-control bypass; predates rows 88-90, found only now because the earlier reviews targeted the persistence walk): the MCP raw tools take `catalog_path: String` and `path: Vec<String>` as two INDEPENDENT arguments (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/generic/rci.rs` ~40-78 `RciReadInput`/`RciWriteInput`; `support/validate.rs` ~60-75 only bounds their lengths). `rci_execute` (`rci.rs` ~195-229) and `rci_delete` (~232-263) validate, risk-classify and decide persistence, the `allow_destructive`/`confirm` gate and the fail-safe arming from `catalog_path`, then hand the caller-chosen `input.path` to the workflow; `ChangeSet::apply` (`crates/kcc-app/src/workflow.rs` ~838-876) re-validates `request.catalog_path`, POSTs to `RciPath::from_segments(&request.rci_path)` and audits `target = catalog_path` with the outcome derived from `catalog_path`'s persistence. Nothing checks that `path` is an instance of `catalog_path`. `rci_read` (~163-192) validates `catalog_path` with Read permission, then `client.show(input.path)` (`crates/kcc-rci/src/engine.rs` ~408-426) tries a GET and on `NotFound` POSTs `{}` to the same path. F2 reproduced on the fake router (`.omo/evidence/F2-keenetic-control-center-r7.log`, addendum "MCP output"; `allow_destructive = false`): `kcc_rci_execute catalog_path="system country" path=[system,reboot] value={}` answers "saved by the router at once: no save is needed" `persists_immediately: true`, audited `router.change system country applied_persisted`, while kcc POSTed `/rci/system/reboot` (control with the honest `catalog_path="system reboot"` is `destructive_disabled`); the same with `path=[system,description]`; and a VIEWER (Read only) calling `kcc_rci_read catalog_path="show version" path=[system,reboot]` makes kcc POST `{}` to `/rci/system/reboot`. Consequences: a `RawRci` holder bypasses the destructive switch and `confirm` and the audit names the wrong command and outcome; a read-only key can issue a POST through a tool annotated `read_only_hint = true` (whether a real router answers 404 to `GET /rci/system/reboot` is UNVERIFIED on hardware: record it in the not-proven list). The CLI (`crates/kcc/src/cli/rci.rs` `path.join(" ")`) and the Web console (`crates/kcc-web/src/advanced/rci_check.rs` `path_segments`) derive the wire path FROM the catalog path, so this is MCP-only. FIX: keep the tool schema (clients depend on it) but make the server authoritative: derive the expected wire segments from `catalog_path` with the same rule the CLI and Web use (put ONE shared derivation where both and MCP can use it, for example a function next to `RciPath` or on `Catalog`, handling object-name instance slots exactly as the catalog/CLI/Web do: read how `kcc rci`, the Web console and `ValidatedRequest` treat them before deciding), and refuse any `path` that does not match with a typed `McpFailure::ValidationFailed` (audited as `refused` / `validation_failed`, nothing sent, zero router requests); in `rci_read`, the GET->POST `show` fallback must never apply to a path that is not exactly the validated catalog `show` command. Then AUDIT EVERY OTHER MCP tool (and the Web/CLI surfaces) for the same shape: two or more independently supplied inputs that must agree but are only validated separately (`host` vs `router`/`source`/`target`, `op` vs its parameters, `path` vs `catalog_path`, `kind` vs method, a batch item's keys vs its declared root, preset/migration plan ids vs the router they were planned for) and fix or document each, listing them in the notepad. Tests, RED FIRST, deterministic (harness `crates/kcc-mcp/tests/support/lab.rs` + fake router request log): per tool (`kcc_rci_read`, `kcc_rci_execute`, `kcc_rci_delete`, and any other tool you find) a mismatched `catalog_path`/`path` returns `validation_failed`, sends ZERO router requests, appends one `refused` audit entry and never claims persistence; the F2 reproductions (`system country` + `[system,reboot]`, `[system,description]`, viewer read + `[system,reboot]`) as explicit cases; the honest matching call still works (all existing raw-tool tests stay green); instance-name slots work for a real catalog command with an object name. Mutation self-check (drop the equality check, see the tests red, restore byte-identical, sha256 before/after in the notepad). (2) F2 N23 (low): `crates/kcc-web/src/page_kit/confirm.rs` ~206 `else if !self.persisted_paths.is_empty() && self.risk == Risk::Write { keys::WEB_EXPLORER_CONFIRM_MIXED_TEXT` survives `&&`->`||`: add dialog assertions for an ordinary Write request (`web-explorer-confirm-write-text`, no mixed text) and a Management-risk mixed request (keeps `web-explorer-confirm-management-text`), on message keys/markers. (3) F2 N25 (low): `redact_secrets -> ()` (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/support/shape.rs` ~137-168) survives every kcc-mcp test: add a `kcc_rci_read` test that reads a `show interface` whose fake projection carries `wpa-psk`/`password` secrets (`crates/kcc-testkit/src/projection/interface.rs` ~54, 69) and asserts `<redacted>` in the MCP result and the secret value absent, with a run-time-built dummy secret planted through the fake. (4) F2 N24 (nit): `PersistencePaths::visit` (`crates/kcc-catalog/src/request.rs` ~123-144) re-checks what the validator just proved (`proven`, the `child.is_object() && children.contains(key)` branch): do NOT change the logic (the catalog-wide property test pins it); add a short comment stating it deliberately guards future callers of `capture` and that the mutants there are equivalent. (5) F2 N21 (low): raw CLI lines the catalog cannot map (for example the abbreviation `sys country DE`) go out unvalidated and are always reported unsaved: state this default in the `kcc_cli_parse` tool description (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/registry/docs.rs` / the tool's description text) and, if generated text changes, regenerate `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` with `cargo xtask gen-tool-docs` (equality test green). - [x] 93. Risk and completion must follow every nested command, not just the request's own path (found by F3 round 8 N25 HIGH, F4 round 8 N22 HIGH and N21, F2 round 8 N28)
  What to do / Must NOT do: (1) HIGH (found independently by F3 N25 and F4 N22; pre-existing since `8166560`, all three surfaces): a destructive or management command NESTED in a root body or in a parent-path value bypasses the destructive gate, the typed confirmation, the `Destructive` permission check and the management fail-safe, because risk is taken from the request's OWN path or the root field names only: `crates/kcc-catalog/src/request.rs` ~60-64 `risk: catalog.risk(path).unwrap_or(Risk::Write)` and `crates/kcc-catalog/src/validate.rs` ~113-117 (root risk = max of `catalog.risk(field)` over the TOP-LEVEL keys only); `catalog.risk` (`runtime.rs` ~108-129, `DESTRUCTIVE_PATHS` in `runtime/data.rs` ~99-110: `system reboot`, `system configuration factory-reset`, `components commit`, `erase`, `media * format`, ...; management prefixes `ip http `, `user `, `interface * ip address|up|down`) checks only the one path it is given. The row-90 persistence walk descends into children; risk never did. Every consumer trusts `validated.risk`: MCP `rci_execute`/`rci_batch` (`crates/kcc-mcp/src/generic/rci.rs` ~221, 229, 292-295: `require_destructive`), the CLI `send` (`crates/kcc/src/cli/rci.rs` ~121-125, 144: `Gate` = needed permissions + confirmation + fail-safe), the Web RCI console (`crates/kcc-web/src/advanced/rci.rs` ~155, 191, `rci_check.rs` ~129). - [x] 116. The recovery message must name the management item's fate, and the CLI `--json` output must carry the armed fact (found by F2 round 14 N58 LOW, F3 round 14 N59 MEDIUM)

  What to do / Must NOT do: (1) F2 N58 (the round-13 N53 class one shape over): the "applied" fact row 112 feeds `recovery::finish` is `applied_changes != 0`, i.e. ANY item succeeded: CLI `kcc/src/cli/rci.rs` ~171, MCP `kcc-mcp/src/generic/rci.rs` ~360, Web `kcc-web/src/page_kit/change.rs` ~409-422. In a batch `[{"ip":{"http":{"port":{"port":8083}}}},{"system":{"description":{...}}}]` where the router REFUSED the management item and accepted the ordinary one, followed by a refused disarm, all three surfaces say "The management change was applied, but its recovery timer could not be disarmed" (`MANAGEMENT_DISARM_FAILED`, MCP `management_applied_fail_safe_armed`); the user looks for the router on a port that never changed (F2 repro: `refuse_batch_pointer("/ip/http/port")` + `fail_parse_matching(<disarm line>, 1)`; `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md` ~20-24 says partial application refers only to successful changes). Fix: make the applied fact about the MANAGEMENT items: `kcc-app/src/workflow_batch.rs` `BatchChangeResult` reports how many items whose validated request `contains_management()` succeeded (a new counter next to `applied_changes`; do not change `applied_changes` itself); `recovery::finish` callers (CLI, MCP, Web) pass `applied = applied_management_changes != 0`; so a refused management item with an applied ordinary item and a refused disarm reports the OUTCOME-UNKNOWN-ARMED wording (the timer is armed; the management change's fate is the refusal) and still lists the per-item results (`responses`, CLI item list, Web panel). You may edit `kcc-domain/src/configuration/recovery.rs` only to add a doc line if needed (the rule's signature stays). - [x] 118. The MCP migration state must release the router lease after a finish and must not be replaced by another plan (found by F4 round 14 N59 MEDIUM, N60 LOW)

  What to do / Must NOT do: (1) F4 N59 (MEDIUM): `kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/state.rs` ~76-84 stores the `Applier` (which owns the `ChangeSet`, i.e. the cross-process router lease released only on `Drop`, `kcc-app/src/workflow.rs` ~732-740) in the in-memory `migrations` map and `finish.rs` ~112-122 calls `commit()`/`rollback()` but keeps the entry; `kcc_preset_apply` records into the same map (`preset_tools/apply.rs` ~79). After `kcc_migration_finish` succeeds the target stays locked for every front end ("router busy: another process" on the CLI exit 5, Web "The router is busy", MCP `conflict`) until the entry is replaced or `kcc serve` restarts (F4 repro `lease-x.log`, `lease-m.log`, `lease-ms.log`: `fuser state/leases/<router>.lock` names the `kcc serve` pid). The Web wizard takes the Applier out of its slot after finishing (`kcc-web/src/migrate_wizard/finish.rs` ~153). Fix: take the Applier out of the map and drop it when a finish (commit or rollback) completes, or fails in a way that closes the run, so the lease is released; keep the entry (and report) when the finish failed with the timer still armed so the caller can retry (row 117 gives `ApplyError` the armed fact). ENUMERATE every other long-lived holder of a `ChangeSet`/`Applier`/lease in `kcc serve` (MCP preset runs, jobs, Web wizard slots, background tasks: `grep -rn "ChangeSet\|Applier" crates --include=*.rs` outside tests) and record in the notepad each holder with the event that releases it and a test or reasoned verdict. (2) F4 N60: the migration map is keyed by a plan id that names neither routers nor caller (`kcc-migrate/src/planner/diff.rs` ~45-75 hashes startup md5, last-change checksums, plan lines; `kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/plan.rs` ~217 `insert(id, migration)` overwrites any existing entry, dropping its `Applier` and orphaning its armed timer): a second `kcc_migration_plan` for the same pair (other key, same principal, or even another router with identical fingerprints) silently replaces an applied, unfinished migration (F4 repro `planid-x.log`, `planid-x-same.log`: report shows `apply: null`, `finish commit` -> `conflict`/`forbidden`, timer left armed). Fix: key the entry by the router (host) AND the id (the id the tool returns may stay the planner's id plus a router component), and REFUSE to replace an entry whose apply has run and has not been finished (`conflict` with a message that tells the caller to finish or roll back the open migration first); a re-plan before any apply may still replace. Red-first tests (`crates/kcc-mcp/tests/`): finish commit then a CLI/Web-equivalent lease probe (the harness exposes whether the lease is free: a second `ChangeSet` acquisition on the same router succeeds), finish rollback, failed finish keeps the entry, re-plan after apply refused, re-plan for another router with identical fingerprints does not collide, both modes; mutation self-check with sha256 lines. Must NOT: change the planner's id derivation used by the CLI/Web (`kcc-migrate/**` stays untouched unless an id helper is strictly needed; justify), permissions, audit format, the bracket, `kcc-mcp/src/generic/**` and `kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/finish.rs`'s ERROR MAPPING (row 117 owns it: rebase on it, change only the entry lifecycle).

- [x] 133. A transient audit-lock timeout must not kill the audit writer, and a failure after the arm must still close the recovery bracket (found by F3 round 16 N70 MEDIUM; pre-existing, exposed by concurrent processes)

  What to do / Must NOT do: F3 N70 (MEDIUM; evidence `.omo/evidence/F3-r16-n70-audit-writer-dead.log`, `F3-r16-n70-mcp-stdio-after-audit-death-rm.log`, `F3-r16-c-refusal-desi.log`, `F3-r16-b3-cli-outcome-trap.log` RB4j; happened twice under 4-6 concurrent kcc processes on one state directory at load average 17-18): `kcc-app/src/audit.rs` ~429-462 `writer_task` runs `receiver.close(); break;` on ANY failed append, including the retryable `AuditError::AppendLockTimeout` of `audit/append_lock.rs` (200 attempts x 10 ms = 2 s), so one transient cross-process lock timeout kills the process's audit writer for its lifetime. Consequences observed: `kcc serve` cannot log anyone in ("audit writer is unavailable", `/readyz` 503 `audit_alive:false`, every Web login re-renders the login page silently, MCP HTTP writes `{"error":"internal"}`; only a restart recovered, the Quadlet unit restarts on exit, not on an unhealthy check); callers append AFTER the router request, so processes with a dead writer keep SENDING router changes whose audit then fails, including arming the recovery timer and leaving it armed (Web console `no interface Bridge0` as `desi`: the router received `system configuration fail-safe timer reboot 300`, the audit append timed out, NO disarm, the page said "The command could not be run. Try again.", `config state` "armed: yes"; MCP stdio: later changes still sent, `{"error":"internal"}`, timer left armed; CLI `--json rci batch` partially applied with no `router.change_batch` audit entry, exit 1). Fix (three parts, keep each small): (1) a lock timeout is TRANSIENT: the writer keeps running (state is restored as today), only that one append fails with the typed error, and the lock wait is more patient for contention (a longer bounded window with backoff/jitter; a cross-process fairness argument in the notepad); genuinely fatal conditions (chain corruption, I/O error on the log, serialization failure) still stop the writer exactly as today (keep the existing semantics and tests for those); (2) refuse BEFORE any router request when the writer is dead: `ChangeSet::begin` (and any other entry that sends router requests before its first audit append: enumerate the CLI `run_change`/`Ctx::target`, MCP funnel, Web `Action::open`/`run_with`, the interface guard, preset/migration `Applier::start`) checks `audit.is_alive()` and fails closed with a typed error that every surface renders truthfully (reuse an existing key such as the service-unavailable/internal ones if one fits; if you add a message add it in six locales); (3) the bracket closes whatever happens after the arm: if the audit append (or any other step) fails after `SafetyNet::arm` succeeded, the shared recovery rule (`kcc-domain/src/configuration/recovery.rs`, call it; do not edit it) must run the disarm or report the armed timer (`fail_safe_armed:true` where an audit entry can still be written; the localized armed wording otherwise) on CLI, MCP and the Web console and explorer. `kcc serve` should also exit non-zero or recover when its writer dies permanently (fatal case) so a supervisor restarts it: choose one and document it in `docs/MONITORING.md`/`docs/SECURITY.md`. Red-first tests: kcc-app (a held cross-process-style lock for longer than one attempt window but shorter than the new window: the append succeeds; a lock held forever: THAT append fails, the writer is still alive and the NEXT append succeeds after release; a fatal error still stops the writer), workflow (`begin` refused with zero router requests when the writer is dead: use the existing test seam that stops the writer), and one integration test per surface (CLI, MCP, Web) where the writer dies between the arm and the first audit append: the disarm/armed report happens, no silent armed timer; no fixed sleeps (inject the clock or the retry parameters). Mutation self-check per behaviour with sha256 lines. Must NOT: change what the bracket sends, the audit entry format or the hash chain, permissions, risk classification, `kcc-catalog/**`, `kcc-migrate/**`, the Web consoles' dialogs, the apply pre-checks; do not weaken the append lock's mutual exclusion (the chain must never fork).
  Closes: GAP-5
  Parallelization: Wave W32 | Blocked by: 129 | Blocks: F1-F4 re-run
  References: F3 round 16 N70 (`.omo/evidence/F3-keenetic-control-center-r16.md`, artifacts `F3-r16-n70-*`).
  Acceptance criteria: G0 green; a transient lock timeout fails one append only and the writer survives; a dead writer refuses before any router request on every entry; a failure after the arm still disarms or reports the armed timer on CLI, MCP and Web; the fatal-writer behaviour is documented.
  QA scenarios: happy - `cargo test -p kcc-app -p kcc-mcp -p kcc-web && cargo test -p kcc --test cli 2>&1 | tee .omo/evidence/task-133-keenetic-control-center.log`; failure - an append that times out on the lock leaves the writer alive and the next append succeeds, appended.
  Commit: Y | fix(app,cli,mcp,web): keep the audit writer alive across lock timeouts and close the recovery bracket when the audit fails after the arm
  Recommended task executor category: deep-low

Ask an administrator." - [x] 135. A one-shot rollback that the router accepted before it stopped answering must report like the explicit rollback (found by F4 round 16 N71 LOW; row-127 residue)

  What to do / Must NOT do: F4 N71: a one-shot `kcc migrate apply ... --finish rollback` (and `preset apply ... --finish rollback`) whose rollback request the router ACCEPTED and answered before it stopped answering (exactly what an extender does after `system reboot`) waits about two minutes (`wait_restored`) and then prints "Migration stopped. Review the result before choosing rollback." (exit 6, `--json` kind `router`, no `fail_safe_armed`) and writes NO `cli.change` audit entry (repro `.omo/evidence/F4-keenetic-control-center-runs-r16/finish127-m-lost.log` lost-O1/lost-Q1, `finish127-ms-lost.log` lost-O1; proxy `.drop after:<rollback body>`); the explicit `migrate finish rollback` for the same router-side outcome says, in extender mode, "The router is restarting with its saved configuration, which may already include the migrated settings." (exit 0) and, in router mode, the armed wording with `fail_safe_armed:true` and a `cli.change failed` audit entry; MCP `kcc_migration_finish rollback` answers `router_unreachable` and keeps the entry. The refused and reply-lost variants of the same one-shot path are correct. Fix: make the one-shot path and the library `Applier::rollback` (`kcc-migrate/src/apply/finish.rs`: after the terminal request, `wait_restored` failing to reach the router is not "migration stopped": it is "rollback sent, router restarting/unreachable") produce ONE outcome per router-side situation shared by the explicit CLI, one-shot CLI, MCP and the Web wizard/preset page: rollback request not accepted -> armed wording (existing); accepted and the router does not answer again within the wait -> the restarting wording (extender: `migrate-rollback-saved`; router mode: the armed/outcome-unknown wording because kcc cannot confirm the native rollback ran); accepted and the router answers -> success; and every path appends its `cli.change`/`web.*`/`mcp.*` audit entry (with `fail_safe_armed:true` where the timer may still be armed). Enumerate the rollback exits of `Applier::rollback`/`wait_restored` and every caller's mapping in the notepad table (extend row 127's table); do NOT change how long the wait is unless the tests need an injectable clock (use the fake clock, no sleeps). Red-first tests: library (`kcc-migrate` finish tests: accepted-then-unreachable in both modes), CLI one-shot (`crates/kcc/tests/cli_finish_recovery.rs`), explicit CLI, MCP, Web wizard and preset page agree on the message key/exit/audit for the same fault; mutation self-check with sha256 lines. Must NOT: change what any bracket sends, permissions, risk classification, the explicit-rollback command choice per mode (row-102 decision), `kcc-domain/src/configuration/recovery.rs`, the apply pre-checks and permission messages (row 134), the MCP state lifecycle (`kcc-mcp/src/migration_tools/{state,plan}.rs`), `kcc-catalog/**`.

APPROVE only if G1 is fully green.

  Recommended task executor category: unspecified-high (load skills `playwright`, `visual-qa`) - start `kcc-fake-router` for KN-1010 and KN-1811 and `kcc serve`; with Playwright MCP walk every Web UI area (fleet, dashboard, each domain page, config/backups, explorer, consoles, migration wizard end-to-end, admin) in all 6 locales at 1280 px light and 390 px dark, capturing screenshots to `.omo/evidence/F3-<area>-<lang>-<theme>.png`; check: no untranslated keys, no overflow/clipping (kk and de strings are longest), keyboard-only navigation of login + one form, focus visible, CSP violations absent in the browser console, SSE updates visible; exercise MCP over stdio and HTTP with an MCP client (rmcp client example or `npx @modelcontextprotocol/inspector --cli`) listing tools and running one read + one guarded write; run three CLI flows (`info`, `clients list --json`, `migrate plan`) plus `kcc copy --groups dns-secure` between the two fake routers and the Web UI preset create/apply wizard; scrape `/metrics` and run `promtool check metrics` on it; start `docker.io/grafana/loki` and `docker.io/library/elasticsearch` (single-node, security on) in rootless Podman, point the log sinks at them, and query the ingested router lines back through their APIs; run the container e2e of todo 57 once more; run `sudo -n kcc --version` (when passwordless sudo exists) and `podman run --user 0` to see both refused. Any defect becomes a new `- [ ] N.` row.
- [ ] F4. Ideal-state fidelity
  Recommended task executor category: unspecified-high - for each IS-1..IS-18 row (one PASS/FAIL row each), re-run the proving scenario named in `## Success criteria` (or read its evidence when it requires hardware and was recorded by todo 51), and state PASS/FAIL with the evidence path in `.omo/evidence/F4-keenetic-control-center.md`; a FAIL becomes a new `- [ ] N.` row, never a note.

## Commit strategy
- Conventional Commits (`feat`, `fix`, `test`, `docs`, `chore`, `ci`, `build`, `refactor`) with crate scopes (`rci`, `catalog`, `config`, `domain`, `migrate`, `app`, `i18n`, `web`, `mcp`, `cli`, `server`, `testkit`, `xtask`, `release`); one commit per todo using the todo's Commit line; commits only when the owner authorized commits for the execution session (otherwise leave changes staged-free in the worktree and report).
- Each commit leaves G0 green; todos that add dependencies also re-run the todo-2 supply-chain checks before committing.
- Generated artifacts (`catalog/5.1/*.json`, `crates/kcc-catalog/src/generated.rs`, `crates/kcc-i18n/src/keys.rs`, `docs/MCP_TOOLS.md`) are committed together with the generator change that produced them; never hand-edited.
- No force-pushes, no history rewrites; PR/branch delivery follows the `$ulw-execute` options chosen by the owner.
